## S1 — The log contract (`R58`, `OR7`)

### What was built

- `infrastructure/observability/{__init__,logging}.py` canonical in `services/orders/src/otc_orders/`, copies (docstring + identical body) in the other five services. `install_json_logging(service)` is idempotent (handler tagged `_otc_json_logging_service`), adds one synchronous `StreamHandler(sys.stderr)` with `structlog.stdlib.ProcessorFormatter`, sets the root to INFO, never removes another root handler, and clears/re-propagates `FOREIGN_HANDLER_LOGGERS = ("uvicorn", "uvicorn.error", "uvicorn.access")`. Pre-chain: `merge_contextvars`, `add_log_level`, `add_logger_name`, `ExtraAdder`, `drop_color_message` (uvicorn's ANSI copy of each message; not in the design, added after the real-process output showed it), `add_trace_context`, `add_timestamp` (through `otc_contracts.wire.format_instant`, from `record.created`), `format_exc_info`; renderer `JSONRenderer(serializer=serialize)` with compact separators and `ensure_ascii=False`. `service` only tags the handler (no `service` key is rendered: it is not one of the design's keys).
- Each `main.py` (six): the import, `install_json_logging("<svc>")` immediately before `app = create_app()`, and the docstring paragraph saying why.
- Binding sites (11): Gateway `CorrelationMiddleware.__call__` (HTTP scope only); Orders / Fulfillment / Billing responders `_serve` (the pre-existing body became `_answer`; Orders parses `x-correlation-id` with `UniqueId.parse`, the other two reuse `optional_correlation_id`; no header or a malformed one -> no binding); Orders / Projector / Notifications consumers `handle` (rest moved to `_process`, bound to `envelope.correlation_id` right after the guard); Orders `SagaCommandDispatcher.dispatch_claimed` (body moved to `_dispatch_claimed`; the fast path and the sweeper both enter through it); `OutboxRelay._cycle` in Orders / Fulfillment / Billing (see deviation 1).
- Tests: `services/orders/tests/unit/test_log_rendering.py`; `tests/architecture/{test_telemetry_wiring,test_correlation_binding_sites,test_observability_copy_parity}.py`; `tests/observability/log_contract_support.py` (shared reader, not a test module); `services/<svc>/tests/integration/test_<svc>_log_contract.py` ×6.
- Existing tests touched: the `FakeMessage` of `services/orders/tests/unit/saga/test_orders_cancel_wire.py`, `test_orders_create_responder.py` and `test_catalog_reference_list.py` gained `headers = None` (nats-py's `Msg.headers` is always an attribute; the new `_serve` reads it); `services/projector/tests/unit/test_projector_dependencies.py` `EXPECTED` gained the three packages (the test's own comment states a new dependency is a deliberate edit there).

### Deviations from `design.md` (also written on the `tasks.md` boxes and in `design.md` 2.4)

1. **Relay binding site.** `design.md` 2.4 says "around the publication of one row"; `OutboxRelay._cycle` publishes a BATCH via `FactPublisher.publish(facts)`, so no per-row publication exists in `relay.py`. The delivered site is `OutboxRelay._cycle`, wrapping each per-row error log emission (the only per-row lines the relay writes). S3.5 adds the per-row `outbox.publish` span and can move the binding with it.
2. **Trace half of S1.6 deferred to S3.** No span exists in any process before S3.6 / S3.10, so a `traceId` equivalence cannot be asserted. The six tests assert the correlation half over every line and that NO line carries a trace key (nothing fabricated). They are named `..._carries_its_correlation_id` (the brief's name ends `..._correlation_id_and_trace_id`; a test's name is a claim). `check_contract(trace_id=...)` already implements the equivalence; S3 re-points the six tests and renames them.
3. **S1.7's "response header".** A Problem response carries no `X-Correlation-Id`: `openapi.yaml` declares it on the two 201 responses only (lines 228 and 722), and no Problem schema declares it. Not added (the spec is silent; the brief says work from the spec). The body's `correlationId` and the failure line's are asserted.
4. **Unit of work per service** (the brief leaves it open where the design says "an RPC request"): Orders `orders.create` with body `{}` (the responder refuses it: the line `orders.create refused: VALIDATION_FAILED ...`), Fulfillment `fulfillment.stock.reserve` and Billing `billing.credit.hold` with no payload (`... request carried no payload`), Gateway `GET /orders` without a token (the library's `uvicorn.access` line), Notifications an `order.placed.v1` golden fact (the console sender's `notification (console adapter)` line, `NOTIFICATIONS_SENDER=console`), Projector an `order.shipped.v1` envelope (the unregistered-type line). Each provoked line is written by code with no explicit correlation argument, so only the binding can put `correlationId` on it.
5. **Projector / Notifications isolation.** The two children consume the REAL fact topics (constants of the service, no seam in a separate process) on the session broker, which holds earlier tests' facts. Item 10's category "commits its group to the end first": a unique consumer group per test, committed at the end of every partition of the three topics before the child starts (`commit_group_at_end` in the support module). S4.1 inherits this module and must classify these two tests in its enumeration.

### S1.1 / S1.2

Files: the five logging copies above; `main.py` ×6. See "What was built".

**(e) probe, both ways** (`/tmp` scratch script, not kept; one active span, the same `build_formatter()` behind each handler):

```
active traceId e46ea99ac72d03b40401545c826f24cb
{"event":"sync handler","level":"info","logger":"probe.sync","traceId":"e46ea99ac72d03b40401545c826f24cb","spanId":"884c29aa3a6ade1b","timestamp":"2026-10-12T15:59:41.521Z"}
{"event":"queue handler","level":"info","logger":"probe.queue","message":"queue handler","timestamp":"2026-10-12T15:59:41.521Z"}
```
The synchronous handler carries the active span's `traceId`; the `QueueHandler`/`QueueListener` variant carries none (and loses the `event` key too: `QueueHandler.prepare` collapses the record before the listener's formatter sees it). Ledger L7 holds both ways.

### S1.6 six real-process tests

`services/<svc>/tests/integration/test_<svc>_log_contract.py`, test name `test_r58_every_line_the_real_server_writes_is_json_and_every_line_of_one_unit_of_work_carries_its_correlation_id` (see deviation 2). The shared contract (`log_contract_support.check_contract`) over EVERY stderr line: one JSON object each; `level`, `logger`, `event`, `timestamp` non-empty strings and the timestamp in the wire instant format; no empty-string value anywhere; `traceId`/`spanId` hex-shaped when present; the JSON `Started server process` line exists; the server's lifecycle lines carry no `correlationId`; each provoked line exists and carries the unit's `correlationId`. The child is spawned with `start_new_session=True`, stopped by SIGTERM, killed by process group on a timeout; every wait is `asyncio.timeout` whose message names the claim, paced at 0.2 s, and fails at once on a non-JSON line.

### Findings and proposed dispositions

1. **Relay / dispatcher / fast-path bindings are masked by explicit `extra={"correlationId": ...}`** (the three sites already passed it, and `test_fast_path.py:275` reads it as a `LogRecord` attribute). A deleted binding there is caught by the census, not by a rendered line. Proposed: accept for S1 (the census is the guard); S3.5 / S4.14 add rendered-line cases at the relay and the dispatcher when their per-row spans exist, and S8.1's walk of L4 must answer "does this guard execute the code the row is about?" with this limit stated.
2. **Root logger now INFO and one JSON stderr handler on the root once any `main` module is imported**, including inside the pytest process (all six `main.py` are imported by existing tests). The full suite is green (4813), so no `caplog`-based test depended on the old root level; but any future test that counts `caplog.records` exhaustively could see library INFO records. Proposed: accept; note for later stages' authors.
3. **`aiokafka.consumer.group_coordinator` ERROR `GroupCoordinatorNotAvailableError`** appears in the Projector / Notifications child's stderr on a first group join against a fresh broker. It is JSON, outside any unit of work, and harmless; the contract tolerates it. Proposed: accept.
4. **`logging.py` as a module name** inside `observability/` shadows nothing (absolute imports) but invites confusion with the stdlib; the design names it. Proposed: keep.
5. Orders' `subjects.py` holds a fourth constant (outbound `STOCK_CHECK_SUBJECT`); S3.6's "15 subjects" test must read the three responder classes' subscriptions, not the file's constants count.
6. S4.1 must classify the two new Kafka-reading real-process tests (Projector, Notifications log contracts) as *commits its group to the end first* in its enumeration.

## S2 — `R62` idempotent replay (2026-10-12)

### Built (files)

- `services/orders/src/otc_orders/application/ports/unit_of_work.py`: `RequestIdAlreadyPlacedError`.
- `.../application/ports/order_repository.py`: `save(order, *, request_id=None)`, `find_by_request_id`.
- `.../infrastructure/persistence/order_repository.py`, `order_mapper.py` (`order_row_of(..., request_id)` writes it; the old `request_id=None` is gone), `unit_of_work.py` (translation after the rollback, by `constraint_name`).
- `.../application/commands/place_order.py`: fast path (RI2) after the order-discount refusal and before reference data; `_place`; the collision caught OUTSIDE the block and resolved by a re-read in a new transaction (RI3); `causation_id = UniqueId(request_id)` when supplied (RI5); `_result_of` is the one projection for both paths.
- Tests: `services/orders/tests/unit/test_place_order_request_id.py` (7), `services/orders/tests/integration/test_orders_create_idempotent_replay.py` (6); `tests/unit/test_place_order_handler.py` fake gained the new port members.
- `tests/architecture/test_order_construction_sites.py`: the literal `("place", "application/commands/place_order.py", "handle")` became `"_place"` (the handler was split; the test failed on the old name in the gate run, which is its own evidence). Outside the Bounds list as written; the minimal consequence of design §3.2, flagged here.
- `specs/shared/test-matrix.md`: R62 Status cell only. `tasks.md` S2.1 – S2.7 ticked with notes.

### S2.1 / S2.3 [MEASURE], verbatim (`postgres:18.6`, throwaway test, deleted)

```
MEASURE request_id: type(error)=<class 'sqlalchemy.exc.IntegrityError'>
  type(error.orig)=<class 'sqlalchemy.dialects.postgresql.asyncpg.AsyncAdapt_asyncpg_dbapi.UniqueViolationError'>
  type(error.orig.__cause__)=<class 'asyncpg.exceptions.UniqueViolationError'>
  cause.constraint_name='uq_orders_request_id'
  cause.sqlstate='23505'
  cause.table_name='orders'
  cause.schema_name='public'
  cause.message='duplicate key value violates unique constraint "uq_orders_request_id"'
  orig.sqlstate='23505' orig.pgcode='23505'
  orig.constraint_name='<absent>'
MEASURE order_reference: (same classes)
  cause.constraint_name='uq_orders_order_reference'  cause.sqlstate='23505'
  cause.message='duplicate key value violates unique constraint "uq_orders_order_reference"'
MEASURE indnullsnotdistinct: [(False, 'uq_orders_request_id')]
MEASURE pg version: PostgreSQL 18.6 (Debian 18.6-1.pgdg13+2) on x86_64-pc-linux-gnu, compiled by gcc (Debian 14.2.0-19) 14.2.0, 64-bit
```

Consequence: `constraint_name` is NOT on `error.orig` (SQLAlchemy's adapter class); it is on `error.orig.__cause__` (asyncpg's). The translation reads that, checked with `isinstance(..., asyncpg.exceptions.UniqueViolationError)`.

### Deviations and findings

1. Barrier arm survived in its first form (above); fixed by making the barrier observable. Disposition: fixed.
2. The existing `test_orders_request_id.py` (raw SQL, `test_r62_*` names) stays: it proves the index, not the flow. No collision of names with the new file.
3. Fast path placement: after the order-discount refusal (a pure validation that needs no data), before reference data. If the reviewer reads RI2 as "even before the discount check", it is a one-line move; the discount refusal could never have been part of an accepted order.
4. A transient `RequestIdAlreadyPlacedError` re-read finding no winner re-raises the ORIGINAL database error (`__cause__`), as the test name says.

## S3 — Tracing (`R56` mechanism, `R57`, `OR4`, `OR5`) (2026-10-12)

Every `[ARM]` below has its backup under `.arm/r27/` (`<path with / as _>.bak`) and its mutation, sha256 and the verbatim failure lines in `.arm/r27/arms.log` (git-ignored, written by `.arm/arm2.sh` for every arm of this pass, failed or not). The table keeps the mutation, the sha256 of the unmutated file and one verbatim failure line; `cmp` was `ok` after every restore (the script prints `restored (cmp ok)` and cleans `__pycache__`).

### What was built (files)

- **Settings (S3.1):** `TelemetrySettings` (`otlp_endpoint`, `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`, default `""`) in the six `infrastructure/settings.py`, built by the six `load_settings` into a new `telemetry` field; `tests/architecture/test_composition_env_reads.py` `EXPECTED_SETTINGS`; the six `test_<svc>_settings_env.py` (ENV / DEFAULTS tables; the Projector test gained a sentinel assertion); root `conftest.py` session fixture `_no_session_exports_telemetry`. Test-built `ProjectorSettings` (Gateway SSE test, Projector integration conftest) gained `telemetry=TelemetrySettings()`.
- **Carrier and providers (S3.2, S3.4), parity family `OBSERVABILITY_MODULES = [logging, telemetry, trace_context]`:** `infrastructure/observability/telemetry.py` (`build_telemetry`, `Telemetry` with `tracer`, `meter`, `shutdown`, `aclose` = shutdown off the loop) and `trace_context.py` (`inject_nats`, `extract_nats`, `kafka_trace_headers`, `extract_kafka`, `current_traceparent`, `context_from_traceparent`, plus `NO_TRACER` and `TRANSACTION_SPAN`), canonical in Orders, copies in the other five. Copies are produced by `.arm/copy_family.py` (docstring + token map); `tests/architecture/test_observability_copy_parity.py` is green.
- **FastAPI native telemetry off (S3.3):** inline `telemetry={...all five False...}` in the six `FastAPI(...)` calls.
- **Outbox chain (S3.5):** `writer.py` stores `current_traceparent()`; `wire.py` `to_publishable_fact(row, trace_headers=())`; `relay.py` `tracer` kwarg (default `NO_TRACER`), `build_prefix(rows, tracer)` starts one `outbox.publish` PRODUCER span per row with a recorded trace (under the restored context, not made current) and injects THAT span's context, `end_spans` ends them after the acknowledgement (error status on abort). Fulfillment and Billing copies refreshed by `.arm/copy_outbox.py` (token map + `ruff format`); `test_outbox_copy_parity.py` 94 passed.
- **Responders (S3.6):** `_serve` of `OrdersCreateResponder`, `StockResponder`, `CreditResponder` extract the caller's context and start the SERVER span `"<subject> serve"` (the single extraction site of each class); `tracer` kwarg.
- **Outbound NATS (S3.7):** CLIENT span + a per-call `inject_nats` into the per-call dict at `nats_rpc_client.py`, `nats_saga_commands.py`, `nats_stock_availability.py` (the stock check sent no headers before; it now sends a dict).
- **Transactions (S3.8):** `writemodel.transaction` INTERNAL span in `SqlAlchemyUnitOfWork.begin` (Orders), `SqlAlchemyStockTransactions.run` (Fulfillment, per attempt), `SqlAlchemyCreditTransactions.run` (Billing), `SqlAlchemyDeliveryLedger._begin` and `.forget` (Notifications).
- **Gateway (S3.9):** `TelemetryMiddleware` in `presentation/asgi.py`, added last (outermost) in `presentation/app.py`; `GatewayRuntime.telemetry`; `start_runtime` builds the providers first.
- **Consume (S3.10):** the three `KafkaFactSubscriber`s wrap `handler(...)` in a CONSUMER span under `extract_kafka(record.headers)`; `Externals.subscriber` of the Projector now takes `(KafkaSettings, Tracer)`.
- **Compositions:** `start_runtime(..., span_processors=(), metric_readers=())` in the six; providers are built first, `stack.push_async_callback(telemetry.aclose)` registered first so they are flushed LAST; every traced component receives `tracer=telemetry.tracer`.
- **Tests added:** `tests/observability/trace_support.py` (typed span readers, `HoldingConnection`, `Receiver`); `services/orders/tests/unit/{test_telemetry (4), test_trace_context (24), test_responder_trace_subjects (4)}.py`; `tests/architecture/test_telemetry_wiring.py` (+ native-telemetry guard, OR5 scan and its sentinel, the session-blank guard, the traced-components guard: 6 parametrised + 1 sentinel); `services/orders/tests/integration/{test_trace_propagation (4), test_orders_responder_trace (2), test_orders_saga_commands_nats_headers_per_call, test_orders_stock_check_nats_headers_per_call, saga/test_saga_trace}.py`; `services/fulfillment/tests/integration/test_stock_responder_trace.py (2)`; `services/billing/tests/integration/test_credit_responder_trace.py (2)`; `services/gateway/tests/integration/{test_gateway_nats_headers_per_call, test_gateway_server_span}.py`. New fixtures: `traced_*_factory` + `TracedHost` in the Orders, Fulfillment and Billing integration conftests; `publish_fact(headers=...)` in the saga conftest.
- **Existing tests changed (all consequences of this stage):** the six log-contract tests (see below); `services/orders/tests/integration/test_orders_host_lifespan.py` (two assertions compared the adapters' constructor kwargs to `{"timeout_ms": N}`; they now assert exactly `{timeout_ms, tracer}` with the same value); `services/gateway/tests/integration/test_gateway_over_real_responders.py` (the old `..._the_replay_is_not_built_yet` pin, whose docstring said it fails when feature 27 builds the replay, is rewritten to `test_an_idempotency_key_is_forwarded_and_a_repeat_returns_the_original_order`; it is the S2 consequence the S2 gate did not meet because only Orders was run); `services/gateway/tests/unit/conftest.py` (`GatewayRuntime` gained `telemetry`); `services/projector/tests/unit/test_composition.py` (the seam takes the tracer; the bundle test is now "four classes"); `services/projector/tests/integration/conftest.py`, `services/gateway/tests/integration/test_gateway_sse_signals.py` (seam signature, `telemetry=`); `services/notifications/tests/integration/conftest.py` (a type annotation).

### The six log-contract tests (S1.6's deferred trace half)

Re-pointed at the trace and renamed (the name claims what it asserts): `test_r58_every_line_the_real_server_writes_is_json_and_every_line_of_one_unit_of_work_carries_its_correlation_id_and_trace_id`, in all six `services/<svc>/tests/integration/test_<svc>_log_contract.py`. Each now sends a `traceparent` (HTTP header for the Gateway, NATS header for Orders/Fulfillment/Billing, Kafka header for Projector/Notifications; `support.publish` gained `headers=`), passes `trace_id=` to `check_contract` (the lines carrying the trace id are exactly the lines carrying the `correlationId`), and asserts a line carries the INBOUND trace id (continuation, not a fresh root). The "no trace key is fabricated" assertions of S1 are gone. Docstrings rewritten. **Arms (all six, one mutation each in its service, ran the ONE test):** the fresh-root mutation (`context=Context()`) in the Orders, Fulfillment and Billing responders and in the Notifications and Projector subscribers, and the removed `TelemetryMiddleware` in the Gateway. All six failed with `AssertionError: the lines carrying the unit's traceId are not the lines carrying its correlationId:` (verbatim, in `.arm/r27/arms.log`, 17:33 – 17:34). Measured fact worth keeping: uvicorn writes the access line from inside the app call, so the Gateway's access line carries the span's ids.

### Deviations from `design.md` (also on the `tasks.md` boxes)

1. `OTEL_PROPAGATORS=b3` -> `baggage` in the environment probe (b3 is not installed; the import itself raised). Ledger L22.
2. `test_responder_trace_subjects.py` lives where the box says (`services/orders/tests/unit/`) and imports the Fulfillment and Billing packages; import-linter's independence contract covers `src` only and the Gateway integration conftest already does this.
3. `Externals.subscriber` (Projector) changed signature to carry the tracer; three test seams edited.
4. The CONSUMER span (design 4.4 "Kafka -> consumer") is built here, in S3, because S3.10's continuation needs it; S4.17(b)'s dead-letter half is still S4.
5. Providers and tests take an in-memory `span_processors=` argument on `start_runtime` (the only seam; the lifespan's own call passes none). The saga test wraps `otc_orders.main.start_runtime` to hand a processor to the runtime's own provider.

### Findings and proposed dispositions

1. **A component built without a tracer silently makes no spans** (`NO_TRACER` defaults). Fixed in this stage: `test_every_traced_component_is_constructed_with_the_runtimes_tracer` (AST over the six composition roots, armed).
2. **Notifications' `forget` transaction span has no test of its own** (the population is by search; `forget` is exercised by S4.19's transient-failure case). Proposed: S4.19 asserts a `writemodel.transaction` span per forget; recorded here so the box is not forgotten.
3. **The `uvicorn.access` line of the Gateway carries the trace id only because uvicorn writes it inside the app call.** Measured by the log-contract pass; if a future uvicorn writes it after the ASGI call returns, the Gateway log-contract test (armed) fails, not silently.
4. **`writemodel.transaction` records `RequestIdAlreadyPlacedError` as an exception event with ERROR status** (it propagates through the `with`). It is an expected business outcome (RI3). Proposed: accept (a replay shows as an errored transaction span that has a sibling re-read span); revisit if the S8 walkthrough finds it noisy.
5. **`SagaCommandBusinessRejectionError` likewise marks the CLIENT span ERROR** for a terminal business "no". Same proposal.
6. **The relay's per-row ERROR lines are still bound by an explicit `extra={"correlationId"}` as well as `bound_contextvars`** (S1 finding 1 stands); not changed here.
7. `design.md` §4.4 says the Gateway span is "SERVER `<METHOD> <route template>`"; an unmatched route (404) keeps the bare method as its name (no template exists). Cardinality is bounded; recorded, not guarded.

### `[LEADER]` items waiting

None in S3. For later stages: S8.4 (`.env.example` / developer `.env`: `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=http://localhost:4318`, `FACT_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3`, `FACT_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS=500`) before S8.3.

## S4a — Retry, dead letter, wiring: S4.1 – S4.9 (2026-10-12)

Scope of this pass: the first half of S4 (`tasks.md` S4.1 – S4.9; design 5.1 – 5.5). S4.10 – S4.23 were not started. Arming backups and logs: `.arm/logs/<arm id>.log` (verbatim runs), `.arm/results.md`, `.arm/r27/s4/` (`arms_a.log`, `arms_b.log`, `arms_files.log`, the `*.xml` junit artifacts), the drivers `.arm/arms_s4a.py` and `.arm/arms_s4a_files.py`. Every arm ran the ONE named test under `setsid --wait timeout` (arm.py: `start_new_session` + `killpg` on timeout), restored by `cp`, compared with `cmp`, cleared `__pycache__`/`.mypy_cache`, and re-ran green.

### What was built (files)

- **Settings (S4.2):** `FactRetrySettings` (`FACT_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` 3, refused outside 1..10; `FACT_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS` 500, refused below 0, messages name the variable) in `services/{orders,projector,notifications}/src/*/infrastructure/settings.py`; the bundles and `load_settings` of the three compositions gained `fact_retry`.
- **The wrapper (S4.3):** `infrastructure/messaging/fact_retry.py` canonical in Orders, copies in the Projector and Notifications (`FactRetry`, `FactRetryPolicy`, `DeadLetter`, the `FactClock` / `DeadLetterPublisher` / `FactLatency` protocols, `NoFactLatency`).
- **The publisher (S4.5):** `infrastructure/messaging/dead_letter_publisher.py` (`KafkaDeadLetterPublisher`, `dead_letter_headers`), canonical + two copies; `request_timeout_ms` 10 000 (S4.4). Projector: `infrastructure/clock.py` (a `SystemClock`, the Projector had none).
- **Wiring (S4.5, S4.8):** `FactProcessor` port in each `application/ports/fact_stream.py`; the three consumer tasks (`presentation/{saga,projector,notification}_facts_consumer.py`) take `retry: FactProcessor` and run the processing inside it with a fresh scope per attempt; the three compositions build the producer (client ids `otc-orders-dlq`, `otc-projector-dlq`, `otc-notifications-dlq`), start it before the consumer task and stop it after; test seams: `start_runtime(fact_topics=, dead_letter_producer_factory=)` in Orders and Notifications, `Externals.dead_letters` in the Projector. The three subscribers' docstrings no longer say "until feature 27".
- **Guards:** `tests/architecture/test_observability_copy_parity.py` (messaging family, `OR2`), `test_kafka_client_confinement.py` (literal + sentinels), `test_kafka_client_ids.py`, `test_composition_env_reads.py` (`EXPECTED_SETTINGS`).
- **Tests added:** `services/orders/tests/unit/test_fact_retry.py` (12), `test_dead_letter_publisher.py` (11); per-consumer wrapper cases in `services/orders/tests/unit/saga/test_saga_facts_consumer.py`, `services/projector/tests/unit/test_projector_facts_consumer.py`, `services/notifications/tests/unit/test_notification_facts_consumer.py`; FactRetrySettings cases in the three `*_settings_env.py` tests; `services/orders/tests/integration/saga/test_saga_dead_letter_composition.py`, `services/notifications/tests/integration/test_notifications_dead_letter_composition.py`, one case in `services/projector/tests/integration/test_projector_lifespan.py`; the Projector composition unit test gained the dead-letter producer's start/stop order (`test_or8_a_dead_letter_producer_that_cannot_start_stops_the_boot_before_the_consumer`).
- **Seams of S4.1:** `tests/observability/dead_letter_support.py` (`PrivateTopics`, `create_private_topics`); `private_topics` fixtures in `services/orders/tests/integration/saga/conftest.py` and `services/notifications/tests/integration/conftest.py`; `saga_harness(topics=, dead_letter_producer_factory=)` (`history="keep"` is honoured with private topics); `notifications_host_factory(topics=, dead_letter_producer_factory=)`; the Projector's `Topics.dead_letters`; root `conftest.py` creates the three real `<topic>.dlq` companions as `kafka-init` does.
- **Packages installed:** none.

### S4.1 enumeration (item 10)

Legend: **PT** private topics (a name unique to the test, created by it) / **CE** commits its group to the end first (or a group unique to the test that starts at the end) / **NH** never reads history (no consumer on a fact topic, or a fake consumer) / **RH** reads history deliberately (an un-grouped reader or a replay, selected by content or by the SO1 claim). "Produces" lines say whether the module puts facts on a REAL fact topic and which kind (valid facts only: nothing in the population publishes a poison fact to a real topic; the poison cases were the three SO9 cases of `test_saga_consumption.py`, now on PT, and none exists elsewhere).

Command (as the brief and the task name it): `grep -rln 'KafkaFactSubscriber\|start_runtime\|saga_harness\|AIOKafkaConsumer\|send_and_wait' services/*/tests tests conftest.py` -> 49 modules when the pass began (listed first; it reads 55 now because S4a's own new modules match it). The pattern misses the modules that PRODUCE through the relay or a publisher helper without any of those names, so a second command, `grep -rln 'KafkaFactPublisher\|kafka_publisher\|AIOKafkaProducer\|\.send(\|OUTBOX_RELAY_ENABLED\|publish_fact\|saga_publisher\|support.publish\|publisher.publish\|produce(' ...`, was run and it added 31 modules (4 of them S4a's own new files); those that touch a real fact topic are listed second, the rest in one line.

Classification: **CE; publishes no poison.** It stays on the real topic by design (the contract is the real server's stderr, and the topic is not configurable); the sibling risk is the reverse one, a poison on the real topic met by this child, which no suite publishes after S4a.

### Teardowns of hosts that join a consumer group (#8 id 74's teardown bullet)

- **Orders (the group name is the constant `orders.saga`, so a host that joins it MUST delete it):** `saga_harness` deletes it in its `finally` unless `cleanup=False`; only `test_saga_consumption.py`'s first lifespan of the SO9 restart case uses that, and the SECOND lifespan of the same test runs with the default cleanup and deletes it (`saga/conftest.py`, the `finally` of `start`). `test_saga_dead_letter_composition.py::test_or8_the_runtime_starts_and_stops_in_two_successive_event_loops` (S4a) deletes it in a `finally` through `broker.delete_group()`. No other Orders module starts the consumer (the census `test_saga_harness_census.py` forbids it; every other Orders host test runs with `SAGA_CONSUMER_ENABLED=false`, the `host_environment` default).
- **Notifications (the group is a setting):** `host_environment` gives every call a UNIQUE group (`notifications-it-<uuid10>`); the log-contract test passes its own unique group; the restart case (`test_notification_idempotency.py:97, 104`) pins one group for two hosts of one test (unique per test); `test_notifications_host_lifespan.py` pins `group-sentinel` with a consumer factory that never assigns. None deletes its group; every one is unique, so nothing is inherited. S4a's two cases use `host_environment`'s unique group.
- **Projector (the group is a setting):** every case names its group (`two-loops-a`, `readiness`, `rebuilt`, `replay-g1`, `dead-letter-bootstrap`, `projector-sse-<suffix>`, `projector-log-contract-<uuid>`...) and runs on topics created under a name unique to the test (`endpoints` / `create_topics`), so a group name reused by another test commits against different topics: nothing is inherited. None deletes its group (committed offsets for topics nobody will read again are harmless).
- **Gateway, Fulfillment, Billing:** no consumer group.

**Verdict.** After S4a no module publishes a poison fact to a real topic: the three that did (the SO9 poison/failing cases of `test_saga_consumption.py`) run on PT. Every module that consumes a real topic does so from a position that is either committed at the end first (the saga harness, the two log-contract tests), a unique group on `latest` (Notifications hosts), or a deliberate replay (SO1). The un-grouped relay readers select by content.



### S4.4 [MEASURE] verbatim (real `apache/kafka:4.3.1`, `KAFKA_AUTO_CREATE_TOPICS_ENABLE=false`, `AIOKafkaProducer(acks="all", enable_idempotence=True)`, `send_and_wait` to a topic that does not exist; throwaway test, deleted; log `.arm/r27/s4/measure_s44.log`)

```
MEASURE kwargs={} class=aiokafka.errors.UnknownTopicOrPartitionError elapsed=40.07s msg=UnknownTopicOrPartitionError()
MEASURE kwargs={'request_timeout_ms': 5000} class=aiokafka.errors.UnknownTopicOrPartitionError elapsed=5.02s msg=UnknownTopicOrPartitionError()
MEASURE kwargs={'request_timeout_ms': 5000, 'max_batch_size': 16384} class=aiokafka.errors.UnknownTopicOrPartitionError elapsed=5.02s msg=UnknownTopicOrPartitionError()
```

The bound is `request_timeout_ms` (default 40 000). The publisher sets 10 000 and the failure is `OR8`'s path (the exception propagates out of `FactRetry`, the subscriber does not commit and redelivers).

Kept temporary-tree sentinels (they are tests, so they run on every suite): `test_observability_copy_parity.py` `test_sentinel_a_pristine_messaging_tree_has_no_violation`, `..._one_character_class_change_in_one_copy_of_each_module_is_named` (×2), `..._one_character_in_the_notifications_publisher_is_named`, `..._a_deleted_messaging_copy_is_named`, `..._a_copy_planted_in_billing_is_named`, `..._a_fourth_declared_consumer_with_no_copy_is_not_the_literal`; `test_kafka_client_confinement.py` `..._a_consumer_import_added_to_a_dead_letter_publisher_is_reported` (×3), `..._a_missing_dead_letter_publisher_is_reported` (×3), `..._a_producer_under_orders_application_is_reported`, `..._an_aliased_producer_in_presentation_is_reported`, `..._a_dead_letter_module_in_another_service_is_reported`.

**Survivors.** One arm survived and is NOT ticked as an arm: `4.3d2-except-base-exception-sleep` (the box's `except BaseException` mutation, run against the CANCELLED-BACK-OFF case). It cannot fail by construction: the `sleep` call is inside the `except Exception` block, not inside the `try`, so a `CancelledError` raised there propagates whatever the clause says. The back-off half of the claim is armed by `4.3d3` (the back-off wrapped in `contextlib.suppress(BaseException)`: `Failed: DID NOT RAISE CancelledError`); the work half by `4.3d` (`except BaseException`: `Failed: DID NOT RAISE CancelledError`). Ledger L10 says so. Two other first forms were weak and were strengthened and re-run: `4.2c` (the first test matched the variable name against the whole rendered error, which pydantic fills with the echoed input; it SURVIVED with the message stripped of the variable, then the assertion moved to `error["msg"]`: `assert 'FACT_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS' in 'Value error, the attempt count must be 1..10, got 0'`) and `4.3a2b` (three attempts cannot tell doubling from linear: a 5-attempt test was added; `[0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0] != [0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]`).

Not armed: S4.1's ARM (see below); S4.2's "set `0` attempts: boot refused" is armed through the settings test (4.2b) rather than a booted service; the log lines of `FactRetry` (WARNING per attempt, ERROR on dead-lettering) have no test of their own (the S4.17 integration cases read them from real lines).

### S4.1 ARM - carried to S4.17

The arm needs a dead-letter test that publishes a poison fact; none exists before S4.17. The seam is delivered and exercised by three cases (the two SO9 rewrites on `private_topics`, the composition cases); `tasks.md` S4.17 now carries the arm and S4.1 stays unticked until it runs.

### Stage gate for this pass (developer stack down; only `otcpy-n8n` running)

- `uv run ruff format --check`: `1117 files already formatted`; `ruff check`: `All checks passed!`; `mypy`: `Success: no issues found in 901 source files` (no new override); `lint-imports`: `Contracts: 13 kept, 0 broken.`; `./init.sh`: exit 0.
- First gate run, `uv run pytest services/orders services/projector services/notifications services/gateway tests/architecture` under `setsid --wait timeout -k 10 3000` (junit `.arm/r27/s4/gate.xml`): `tests="3062" failures="4"`, 572 s. The four failures were consequences of this pass and are fixed below.
- Second gate run, `uv run pytest services/orders services/projector services/notifications services/gateway services/fulfillment services/billing tests` (junit `.arm/r27/s4/gate2.xml`): `tests="4202" failures="1" errors="0" skipped="0"`, 590 s (0.141 s per test). The failure, `services/billing/tests/unit/test_no_internal_payment_timer.py::test_every_scheduling_construct_in_the_sources_is_a_classified_retry_pacing`, is a consequence of S4.5 (the three compositions now hand `FactRetry` `asyncio.sleep`; the guard's literal classifies every scheduling construct): the three hits were added to its literal with their reason; the file re-run: `5 passed`. Net after the fix: 4202 tests, 0 known failures. Not re-run as a whole after that one-line change.
- After the `tasks.md` / `design.md` edits and the Billing literal: `uv run pytest tests` (architecture, parity, observability guards; junit `.arm/r27/s4/final_tests.xml`): `tests="651" failures="0"`, 112 s; `./init.sh`: exit 0.
- `test-matrix.md`: no row flipped (the brief's instruction).

### Forced consequences of S4.8 outside the box list (each fixed in this pass, not deferred)

1. **`test_saga_consumption.py`** (3 cases) could not pass with the wrapper: the poison case asserts "not committed and retried" (R16 makes that false), and the two `failing_once_for` cases are absorbed by the in-process retry (#8 id 94's warning, predicted by design 5.8). Done: the poison case is retired (its mutation, validating the payload in presentation, moves to S4.17's Orders case), the SO9 cases are rewritten under the design 5.8 names (`test_so9_a_fact_whose_dead_letter_is_not_durable_leaves_the_committed_offset_unchanged_and_a_restarted_consumer_redelivers_it`, `test_so9_a_failed_record_is_never_committed_past_while_its_dead_letter_is_not_durable`) on private topics with a dead-letter producer the test replaces (it refuses every publication). The first starts with a baseline fact on the same partition so the committed offset is a real position, holds the in-flight record past the 5 s auto-commit window as before, and counts the redelivery as exactly one more attempt. Result: `11 passed` (`.arm/r27/s4/fix.xml`) for that file and the 451 case. **This is S4.20's test-rewrite half; S4.20's four re-run mutations and the 3.12 re-run were NOT done, and S4.20 stays open.** Because the cases are armed by no one yet, a reviewer should treat them as unarmed until S4.20.
2. **`test_notification_send_failure_real_smtp.py::test_a_transient_451_...`** waited for the subscriber's "fact handler failed" line, which is now only emitted when the dead-letter publication fails. It now waits for `fact processing attempt failed` and widens the first back-off to 4 s (`FACT_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS`), so the test observes the failed attempt, clears the chaos, and the SECOND attempt delivers. The dead-letter and permanent-failure cases remain S4.19.
3. **`test_notifications_consumes_only.py`** (NS9) forbade `AIOKafkaProducer` anywhere in Notifications: the dead-letter publisher is the one producer now, allowed in exactly one module (`infrastructure/messaging/dead_letter_publisher.py`), asserted to be the only holder, with a sentinel that the allow-list is a path and not a pattern. The claim "emits no fact" stands (a dead letter is the original bytes on a `.dlq`, not a fact).
4. **Projector `test_composition.py`** (PR43 order): the boot order gained `dead_letters.start` before `consumer.start`, the stop order `dead_letters.stop` after the consumer, and a case that a producer that cannot start stops the boot before the consumer.
5. **Billing `test_no_internal_payment_timer.py`**: see the gate.
6. Settings bundles: the Projector integration conftest and the Gateway's SSE test, which build `ProjectorSettings` by hand, gained `fact_retry`.

### Deviations from `design.md` (also on the `tasks.md` boxes and in design 5.3 / 5.4 / ledger L10, L13, L20)

1. The presentation task holds the wrapper through a port (`FactProcessor`), not the concrete `FactRetry`.
2. `FactRetry` takes the consumer as a `str` and a local `FactClock` protocol (the Projector has no clock port; it gained a one-class `clock.py`).
3. Seams: keyword arguments of `start_runtime` in Orders and Notifications, `Externals.dead_letters` in the Projector.
4. In Orders the dead-letter producer is started only when `SAGA_CONSUMER_ENABLED` is true: existing host tests boot with `KAFKA_BROKERS=127.0.0.1:1` and the relay off. S4.14's first-park hook needs the publisher regardless of the consumer flag and will decide then.
5. The box's linear-backoff and `except BaseException` (back-off) arms could not fail as worded (see Survivors); replaced by arms that can.
6. The Orders dead-letter composition cases sit in `integration/saga/` (the `private_topics` fixture and `broker.delete_group()` live in that conftest; a conftest function is not importable by name under importlib mode).
7. Python questions asked of the port: integer division - none in money (`backoff_ms * (1 << n) / 1000` is a duration in seconds, `[0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]` asserted exactly); JSON serialisation - the wrapper and publisher write no JSON (the value is the original bytes, asserted by a non-canonical fixture); event-loop affinity - the producer is built in `start()`, two-successive-loops cases for all three; cancellation - `except Exception` only, armed for the work half and, by a different mutation, the back-off half; typing gaps - a private `Protocol` over the aiokafka producer, no new `[[tool.mypy.overrides]]`.

### Incident (mine, recovered)

The first version of `.arm/arms_s4a_files.py` ended the Billing "plant a copy" arm with `shutil.rmtree(services/billing/src/otc_billing/infrastructure/messaging)`: that directory EXISTS in Billing (`__init__.py`, `subjects.py`, tracked) and was deleted with the planted file. `uv run mypy` caught it (`Cannot find implementation or library stub for module named "otc_billing.infrastructure.messaging.subjects"`); both files were restored with `git show HEAD:<path> > <path>` (read-only git) and `git status` shows the directory clean again; the `rmtree` line is gone from the script (the arm function already removes only the planted file). The gate runs came after the repair.

### Findings and proposed dispositions

1. **A shutdown during a back-off waits for it.** `sleep=asyncio.sleep`; the stop signal does not interrupt it. Default worst case 1.5 s; the setting's extreme (10 attempts at 500 ms) is 255 s, and Orders' stage stop has no grace (`_stop_stages` awaits the task). Proposed: accept for the defaults and record; if wanted, a stop-aware sleep that raises a dedicated exception (not a `CancelledError`) is a small follow-up, but its log line would read as a handler failure. Re-open trigger: a shutdown measured slower than a back-off in the S8 walkthrough.
2. **`FACT_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` x `FACT_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS` can hold a partition for minutes** (the same arithmetic) while `max_poll_interval_ms` is 300 000: at the extreme the consumer would be evicted from its group mid-retry. Proposed: refuse the combination at boot when the worst-case sum exceeds a bound (for example 60 s), as `SagaSettings` does for the lease; not done (not in the spec). Needs a gate decision, so it is stated rather than built.
3. **The Projector's and Notifications' log-contract tests stay on the real topics** (S4.1): the poison risk is only the reverse one, and no suite publishes one after this pass. A future test that does must use private topics; the architecture has no guard that FORBIDS publishing a poison to a real topic (the population is by convention and by this enumeration). Proposed: a census over the test trees for `payload={` published to the real topic constants is possible but heuristic; not built.
4. **`request_timeout_ms` 10 000** makes a missing `.dlq` stall a partition for 10 s per redelivery round, paced by the subscriber (1 s doubling). Acceptable: `kafka-init` creates the companions, and the root conftest now does too.
5. The unit test of `FactRetry` proves the loop with a fake clock; the real clock's millisecond truncation (`wire_instant`) makes two readings inside one millisecond equal. `x-first-failed-at` and `x-failed-at` can therefore be equal after several fast attempts. That matches `asyncapi.yaml` ("equals only when a single attempt was made" describes the single-attempt case; a sub-millisecond multi-attempt run is theoretical at the default 500 ms back-off). Recorded, not guarded.

### `[LEADER]` items waiting

None in S4a. For later: S8.4 (`.env.example` / developer `.env`: `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`, `FACT_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `FACT_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS`) before S8.3.

New modules, all private topics or no broker: `test_saga_first_park_dead_letter.py` (the concurrency case touches only PostgreSQL; the other two use `private_topics`), `test_operator_cancel_park.py` (PT; reads the real `otc.orders.facts.v1.dlq` read-only, by content), `test_orders_dead_letter.py` (PT), `test_projector_dead_letter.py` (PT through `Topics`), `test_notifications_dead_letter.py` (PT, unique groups). `test_saga_trace.py`'s two new cases start the saga harness with its default `history="skip"` (CE) and publish no fact. **Not armed, and why.** The deadlock statement (finding 4); the three `Refusing` doubles; `test_r29_a_dead_letter_that_cannot_be_published...` was armed by `4.14e` only (move the publish inside the transaction); `FactRetryStopped` raised by a real stop at the SAME instant the timeout fires (finding 7); the first-park log lines have no test of their own (the hook's behaviour is asserted, not its wording). S4.10's migration lifecycle test (upgrade from `0002`, downgrade to `0002`) was not separately armed: `4.10` armed the models-match test, and the lifecycle test asserts the closed four-column set, which `4.10`'s mutation (a column dropped from the migration) would also change.

### The redelivery sentences (S4.20, S4.22; design 5.8)

Each names the Kafka redelivery (or the lack of one) that turned the mutation red.

- **4.20a commit before the handler** (first SO9 case): the record is committed before its handler runs, so the offset the BROKER reports moves to `poison + 1` while the handler is still held (`the offset moved while the handler ran`); the redelivery the case relies on, lifespan 2's new consumer reading from the group's committed offset, would find nothing to redeliver.
- **4.20b `enable_auto_commit=True`, explicit commit removed** (first SO9 case): aiokafka's own auto-commit commits the fetch position, already past the held record, inside the 5 s window the case holds the record for (`the offset moved while the handler ran`): the same lost redelivery as 4.20a, through the client instead of through our code.
- **4.20c no `seek`** (second SO9 case): aiokafka's fetch position is already past a record once it was returned, so without the seek the next poll hands out the FOLLOWING record or nothing; the failed record is not delivered again and the case counts `only 1 refused dead letters in 60 s (3 attempts)` where the seek-and-redeliver loop of the subscriber produces at least 3.
- **4.20d `auto_offset_reset="latest"`** (`test_so1_first_boot...`, re-run unchanged first: green): a first boot of a group with no committed offset starts at the END of each partition, so the fact published before the group existed is never delivered (`timed out after 20s waiting for: the credit.hold row exists`).
- **4.22a** (`test_pr38_a_raising_handler_leaves_the_committed_offset_unchanged_read_from_the_broker`, Projector subscriber committing before the handler): the first, raising delivery is committed (`an offset was committed although the handler raised`), so the redelivery that follows a failure is moot.
- **4.22b** (S4.18's `test_or8_...` on the real lifespan, same mutation): the refused dead letter was supposed to leave `poison` uncommitted for the restarted consumer; the broker reports `2 == (0 + 1)` (`the committed offset (read from the broker) is unchanged: the poison is not committed`), because the commit preceded the handler.

No arm survived. The four SO9 / SO1 cases were NOT edited in this pass: they are the S4a rewrites, now armed.

### F1 and F2 (leader decisions)

- **Ledger row L41** (`design.md` 11): both citations read in the checkouts before writing. #7: `/home/juanpabloperez/Work/Projects/Assessments/order-to-cash-nestjs/apps/notifications/src/infrastructure/messaging/fact-retry-dispatcher.ts:55` is `REAL_DELAY` (`new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms))`, nothing can end it) and `:140` is `await this.delay.for(this.policy.backoffBaseMs * 2 ** (attempt - 1))`. #8: `/home/juanpabloperez/Work/Projects/Assessments/order-to-cash-dotnet/src/Orders/Infrastructure/Messaging/FactRetryDispatcher.cs:112` is `await delay.DelayAsync(backoffMs << (attempt - 1), cancellationToken)` and `:87-92` is `catch (OperationCanceledException) { ... throw; }` ("rethrown unconditionally", ledger L13); the Projector's and Notifications' copies are identical at the same lines (`grep -n 'DelayAsync' src/*/Infrastructure/Messaging/FactRetryDispatcher.cs`). In #9 the property is hand-built by `stop_aware_sleep` (`asyncio.timeout(seconds)` around `stop.wait()`), `FactRetryStopped`, and one `except FactRetryStopped` before `except Exception` in each subscriber; a `CancelledError` still propagates (unit: `test_f1_an_external_cancellation_of_the_stop_aware_sleep_propagates_as_cancelled_error`).
- **The unaware stop was measured by the arm**, not assumed: with the sleep left as `asyncio.sleep`, a stop during a 30 s back-off took 89.7 s in Orders (the harness waited out the whole 30 s + 60 s of the remaining back-offs) and 30.0 s in the Projector and Notifications (their stop grace is 30 s, after which the task is cancelled).
- **F2:** `python -c "import inspect, aiokafka; ..."` -> `max_poll_interval_ms=300000`; the bound is that number, now configured explicitly by the three subscribers from `CONSUMER_MAX_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` (their option dicts are asserted literally with `300000`). The worst case is `backoff x (2^(attempts-1) - 1)` ms, integer shifts only (no `/`, no `Decimal`); the shipped defaults (3 x 500 -> 1 500 ms) and the extreme (10 x 500 -> 255 500 ms) are accepted, 587 ms x 10 (299 957 ms) is accepted and 588 ms x 10 (300 468 ms) is refused.

### Python questions asked of the port

Integer arithmetic: the F2 sum and the doubling use `1 << n`, the duration handed to `asyncio` is `ms / 1000` (a duration, not money); JSON: no dead letter is serialised (the stored `bytea` and the dead-letter value are the received bytes; the tests use bytes no serializer would write); event-loop affinity: the dead-letter producer is built in `start()` inside the lifespan's loop, the two-successive-loops cases (S4a) still pass after the composition move; cancellation: `FactRetryStopped` is an `Exception` subclass raised from inside the `except Exception` handler of `process`, `CancelledError` untouched (unit and armed); typing gaps: the test seams are `ModuleType` (`Any` on attribute access) and every `Any` is stopped at a typed local or a `bool(...)`; no `type: ignore` was added to `src`.

### Deviations from `design.md` (also on the boxes and in design 5.3 / 5.6)

1. First-park hook: the order row is locked BEFORE the claim (deadlock avoidance against every other writer of the pair); the dead-letter producer starts whenever the runtime has a Kafka role (`OUTBOX_RELAY_ENABLED` or `SAGA_CONSUMER_ENABLED`), after the relay's publisher and before the saga's tasks. A first version started it at the top of the boot sequence, before the responder task: `test_a_boot_that_fails_after_the_responder_task_exists_awaited_it_to_completion` (its premise is "the boot fails AFTER the responder task exists") failed in the first full gate run and the start moved back below the relay, which restored the premise.
2. S4.16: the handler passes no traceparent; the queue records the current one (asserted on the stored row, S4.15).
3. S4.15: the absence is read on the three private `.dlq` companions AND the real `otc.orders.facts.v1.dlq`, by value and by key.
4. S4.17: attempts are counted by wrapping `build_saga_fact` (Orders), `parse_fact` (Projector) and `from_wire_json` of the notified model (Notifications); `x-error` is computed in the test by running the same validation on the same bytes.
5. S4.1: the arm's check is injected by the mutation (`ISOLATION: ...`) because the unmutated test has no reason to look for a decoy it never publishes.
6. The `FactRetry` composition seam: the Orders `consumer_stop` event is created before `FactRetry`; Projector and Notifications create `stop_event` before it.
7. The S4a Billing literal entries for `asyncio.sleep` in three compositions are gone (the back-off is no longer `asyncio.sleep` there).

### Findings and proposed dispositions

1. **My first S4.14 second-park assertion could not see a late emission** (the park is committed BEFORE the hook runs, so "the row is parked again" is not evidence the hook has finished). The first arm run of `4.14a` (claim without `IS NULL`) failed on a DIFFERENT assertion (a second dead letter), not on the outbox count. Fixed in this pass: the test wraps the production hook with a completion counter and waits for the second hook to return; `4.14a` then failed on `the claim is made once and never rewritten`. Same pattern in `test_operator_cancel_park.py`.
2. **`services/.mypy_cache` (created by my own `cd` into the wrong directory) failed eight architecture guards** (`test_import_contract_coverage`, `test_money_guard`: a dot-directory under `services/` joined their populations; defeat-list row 10). Removed. Proposed: those two guards enumerate `services/*` and could skip dot-directories; they are outside this feature's Bounds, so this is a proposal, not an edit.
3. **`specs/shared/requirements.md:581` says "all thirteen payloads"** (fourteen with `order.saga_failed.v1`): a stale count in a read-only file. Proposed: an `SA-n` candidate for the leader, or accept (it is a pass-B roadmap sentence).
4. **Deadlock window** between the first-park hook and a fact's handler existed in the design's order (claim, then order lock); removed by locking the order first (deviation 1). Not armed (a deadlock needs a sustained overlap the tests do not create); recorded as a statement.
5. **A runtime with neither `OUTBOX_RELAY_ENABLED` nor `SAGA_CONSUMER_ENABLED` cannot dead-letter its first park** (no producer): the park still appends `order.saga_failed.v1`, the bytes stay in the row, and an ERROR names the row. That is the shape of the existing host tests, not of a deployment.
6. **`Refusing` (a dead-letter producer that never acknowledges) is written three times** in the three new dead-letter test files (importlib mode forbids sharing a conftest function by name). Proposed: accept; `dead_letter_support.py` could carry it if a fourth appears.
7. **`stop_aware_sleep` when the timeout and the stop signal coincide** returns normally or raises `FactRetryStopped`; both are correct (the next attempt either runs for a stopping consumer or the record is redelivered), and neither commits.
8. **The forget transaction span is asserted by COUNT** (six `writemodel.transaction` spans under one consume span: three `record`, three `forget`) because both are named alike (design 4.4). A third kind of transaction in `dispatch` would change the six. Armed by renaming the forget span (`found 3`).
9. **Relay and dispatcher bindings masked by explicit `extra={"correlationId": ...}`** (S1 finding 1) is unchanged; the new first-park log lines carry `rowId` and are inside the dispatcher's `bound_contextvars`.

Arming backups are in `.arm/r27/s5/` (sha256 beside each arm). Every run was under `setsid --wait timeout -k 10 <s> uv run pytest ...`, the result read from pytest's summary line or the junit artifact.

## What was built

- **`infrastructure/observability/metrics.py`** (parity family: Orders canonical + five copies, `OBSERVABILITY_MODULES` gained `metrics.py`): five name constants, five factories, **no unit on any instrument**, a `Gauge` Protocol (the API exports the synchronous gauge only as private `opentelemetry.metrics._Gauge`, 1.45.0), and the adapters `FactLatencyHistogram` (the `FactLatency` port) and `SagaCompletionHistogram` (the `SagaMetrics` port).
- **S5.2** `FactLatencyHistogram(fact_processing_latency(telemetry.meter))` replaces `NoFactLatency()` in the Orders, Projector and Notifications compositions. (`FactRetry` already computed `/ timedelta(milliseconds=1)`; S4 wrote the math, S5 proves it and binds it.)
- **S5.3** Gateway `TelemetryMiddleware` records `otc_request_latency_ms` in its `finally` (error path included); histogram created once per runtime from `telemetry.meter` (cached on the middleware by runtime identity); duration from `time.perf_counter()` (a duration, not a wall instant).
- **S5.4** `OutboxRelay(lag=...)` sets `otc_outbox_lag_ms` at the start of `run_once` (own short session, `SELECT min(occurred_at) ... WHERE published_at IS NULL`, `0` when none). Fulfillment and Billing copies regenerated through `test_outbox_copy_parity.expected_body` (the instrument's own function), then `ruff format`; `test_outbox_copy_parity.py` 94 passed.

- **S5.6** `infrastructure/messaging/dlq_depth.py`: `KafkaDlqDepth` (admin `describe_topics` + group-less consumer offsets) and `DlqDepthTask` (reader started inside the task, in the lifespan's loop; a start failure ends the task). Composition: stage `dlq-depth` appended after the relay's stage; `DLQ_DEPTH_CLIENT_ID = "otc-orders-dlq-depth"`; test seam `dlq_depth_consumer_factory`.
- **S5.8** `tests/observability/test_prometheus_series_names.py`.

## `[MEASURE]` S5.3: is `scope["route"]` visible in the outer middleware after the inner call?

Yes (Starlette 1.7.0 / FastAPI, the real middleware stack): the router stores the matched route in the very scope dict the outer middleware holds. Evidence is the three endpoint values of `test_r59_a_success_an_error_and_an_unmatched_path_...`: `{"GET /orders", "GET /orders/{id}", "unmatched"}` (two different order ids → one value, `GET /orders/{id}`). With the raw path as label the arm fails with `endpoint values were ['GET /no/such/path', 'GET /orders', 'GET /orders/a96fd2a3-...']`.

## Deviations

1. **`dlq_depth.py` uses an admin client for the partitions** (design 6.2 says a group-less consumer's `beginning_offsets` / `end_offsets`). Measured: `AIOKafkaConsumer.partitions_for_topic` is `None` for a topic the consumer was not told about (a first draft therefore reported 0 for every topic, caught by the integration test), and `consumer.topics()` returns a throw-away `ClusterMetadata`, not the client's own. `AIOKafkaAdminClient.describe_topics` answers `error_code: 3` with `partitions: []` for a missing topic **and for a topic created a moment before** (propagation lag, measured right after `create_topics`), so a just-created `.dlq` reports 0 until the asked broker knows it. Written on the design and on the tasks box. The consumer keeps `otc-orders-dlq-depth`; the admin client uses `otc-orders-dlq-depth-admin`.
2. **A `Gauge` Protocol in `metrics.py`** instead of the API's class (private `_Gauge`).
3. **Lag is read at the start of `run_once`, not after the commit**: "0 after the drain" is therefore visible on the NEXT cycle, as design 6.2 says ("at the start of each cycle").
4. The `waits(...)` message of my first DLQ test interpolated the gauge **at call time** (it printed the initial `{}`); removed, because a message that looks like evidence and is not is worse than none.

## Findings and proposed dispositions

1. **S5.5 third arm ("record before the dedup check") cannot isolate the claim.** A duplicate never runs `work`, so `closed` is `None` for it whatever the order of the lines; the arm fails only because it double-records processed facts. The dedup claim is carried by `test_r59_a_deduplicated_redelivery_of_a_closing_fact_records_nothing` and the commit-failure test, both of which would fail if the metric were computed outside `work` from the loaded order. Proposed: accept, with this sentence.
2. **A just-created or never-created `.dlq` both report `0`** (deviation 1). In production `kafka-init` creates the companions, so the window is boot time only. Proposed: accept.
3. **A broker that is down leaves `otc_dlq_depth` at its last value** (the cycle logs `dead-letter depth cycle failed` and sets nothing). Reporting 0 would hide an outage; a stale gauge hides it too, until the task or readiness notices. S6's Kafka probe is what makes the outage visible. Proposed: accept; revisit in S6.
4. **`GatewayRuntime` carries no instrument**; the middleware derives it from `telemetry.meter` once per runtime. Proposed: accept (no change to the frozen runtime or the harness).
5. **`telemetry.meter`'s `create_gauge` is exposed by the API as private `_Gauge`.** If 1.46 renames it, `metrics.py` is unaffected (own Protocol) but the factory's real return type changes; `test_metric_instruments.py` would show it.

# S6 -- Readiness against paused containers (`R60`, `OR6`; design 7) -- 2026-10-13

## What was built

**Health family** (`infrastructure/health/`, design 10; five modules, five populations):

| Module | Canonical | Copies |
|---|---|---|
| `checks.py` (`HealthCheck`, `CheckResult`, `dead_tasks`, `run_checks`, `health_body`) | Orders | Fulfillment, Billing, Notifications, Projector; the Gateway's is `infrastructure/health_checks.py` |
| `postgres_probe.py` (`PostgresProbe`: `SELECT 1`, `NullPool`, `connect_args={"timeout": ...}`) | Orders | Fulfillment, Billing, Notifications |
| `nats_probe.py` (`NatsProbe`: `is_connected`, then `flush(timeout=)`) | Orders | Fulfillment, Billing, Projector, Gateway |
| `kafka_probe.py` (`KafkaAdminProbe`: long-lived `AIOKafkaAdminClient`, `describe_cluster`) | Orders | Notifications, Projector |
| `mongo_probe.py` (`MongoProbe`: `ping`) | Projector | Gateway |

14 copies in all; the Gateway's `health_checks.py` is the fifteenth file. Compositions: `build_health_checks(...)` in each non-Gateway `composition.py` (the attribution of tasks to checks, testable without a container), `readiness()` on the five runtimes replaces `unready_reasons()`, probes closed by the runtime's `AsyncExitStack`; Orders gained an `engine` attribute on its runtime (the write test needs the service's own pool). Presentation: the five `presentation/app.py` answer `HealthResponse` through `to_wire_json` (`/health/live` `200 {"status":"up"}`, `/health/ready` `200`/`503` with one entry per check). The Gateway's `routes_ops.py` now uses `run_checks`/`health_body` and its composition `NatsProbe`/`MongoProbe`. Check names: Orders `writeModel`, `rpcTransport`, `factStream` (no `factStream` for a runtime with neither relay nor consumer); Fulfillment and Billing `writeModel`, `rpcTransport`; Notifications `writeModel`, `factStream`; Projector `readModel`, `factStream`, `rpcTransport`; Gateway `rpcTransport`, `readModel`, `streamSignals`.

**Tests** (all new unless marked):

| Path | What it holds |
|---|---|
| `services/orders/tests/unit/test_health_probes.py` | S6.2: PostgreSQL (a real silent TCP server), Kafka (bootstrap silent / started client silent / one client across a cancelled call), NATS (silent PONG / not connected), `HealthCheck` bound, two hung checks cost one bound, shutting down, dead task attribution |
| `services/projector/tests/unit/test_mongo_probe.py` | S6.2: Mongo `ping` that never answers |
| `services/{orders,fulfillment,billing,notifications,projector}/tests/unit/test_<svc>_readiness.py` | S6.4: liveness consults nothing; 200 with every check up; 503 naming only the failing check, once per check position; each task's death attributed to its check; stopping; (Orders) no Kafka role; (Notifications, Projector) consumer not joined |
| `tests/architecture/test_health_probe_bounds.py` | S6.6: AST census of the 14 probe classes and the 15 `HealthCheck(...)` constructions as literals, every `__call__` await inside a live `asyncio.timeout(self._bound)`, `connect_args` timeout and `flush(timeout=)`, `HealthCheck.run` bounded, one `.probe(...)` call site per copy of `checks.py`; 12 sentinels |
| `tests/architecture/test_observability_copy_parity.py` (extended) | the health family: five populations, the Gateway's `health_checks.py`, census of every `health/` directory, 5 sentinel groups |
| `tests/architecture/test_pause_confinement.py` | S6.7: the only `.pause()` / `.unpause()` calls in the tests are `health_support.paused`'s; the `unpause` is inside a `finally` |
| `tests/observability/health_support.py` + `test_health_support.py` | private containers, `paused`, `poll` (paced), `observe_pause`; the `finally` and the pacing are tested (see "Unpause guarantee") |
| `services/<six>/tests/integration/test_<svc>_health_probes.py` | S6.7: `test_r60_readiness_reports_the_paused_dependency_down_within_its_bound_while_liveness_answers_throughout_and_recovers`, parametrized by dependency: Orders 3, Fulfillment 2, Billing 2, Notifications 2, Projector 3, Gateway 2 = 14 cases |

Changed (S6.5, each changed assertion): `test_{orders,fulfillment,billing,notifications,projector}_health.py` `{"status": "live"}` -> `{"status": "up"}` (and Orders' not-started body `{"status":"unready","reasons":[...]}` -> `{"status": "down", "checks": {}}`); `test_projector_health.py` rewritten for `readiness()` (the fake runtime returns `CheckResult`s; the 503 case names one check); the host lifespan tests `test_{orders,fulfillment,billing}_host_lifespan.py` (200 case: `response.json()["status"] == "up"`; dead task: `down_checks(response) == {check: "transport task X is not running"}` with the task parametrized by the check it serves; closed NATS: `{"rpcTransport": "NatsNotConnectedError"}`; not started: `{"status":"down","checks":{}}`), `test_saga_lifespan.py` (task -> check), `test_notifications_host_lifespan.py` and `test_projector_lifespan.py` (dead consumer: only `factStream` is named and its detail contains the task reason; a cancelled consumer also leaves `subscriber.ready` false, so the detail carries both reasons joined by `; `, as the old `reasons` list carried both); `test_gateway_composition.py` (the fake NATS connection's `flush` takes the `timeout` argument the probe now passes; a not-connected client is `NatsNotConnectedError`, was `ConnectionError`); the four `unready_reasons()` callers (`test_saga_dead_letter_composition.py` x2, `test_notifications_dead_letter_composition.py` x2, `test_projector_lifespan.py`, `test_composition.py` PR43: with fake externals only the task-liveness half is asserted); `test_kafka_client_confinement.py` (the three admin probes join the literal, a probe may name no producer or consumer class, 7 sentinel cases), `test_kafka_client_ids.py` (`otc-{orders,notifications,projector}-health`), `test_write_path_population.py` (`postgres_probe.py` is a classified read-only entry in four services), `test_gateway_degraded.py` (its raw `container.pause()` / `unpause()` now go through `paused`).

## S6.3 [MEASURE] -- Kafka admin probe recovery after a pause longer than the bound

Private `apache/kafka:4.3.1` broker (`health_support.private_kafka`, auto-creation off), a long-lived `AIOKafkaAdminClient`, `describe_cluster()` under `asyncio.timeout(2.0)`. Script in the session scratchpad, not kept in the repository. Output verbatim:

```
MODE reuse
  before pause: up in 0.01s
  paused call 1: down TimeoutError in 2.00s
  paused call 2: down TimeoutError in 2.00s
unpaused
  after unpause call 1: up in 0.00s
up at call 1
MODE percall
  before pause: up in 0.00s
  paused call 1: down TimeoutError in 2.00s
  paused call 2: down TimeoutError in 2.00s
unpaused
  percall 1: up in 0.01s
up at call 1
```

Second run (the same script with the pause held 25 s after the two bounded calls, about 29 s paused in all): `MODE reuse` -> `before pause: up in 0.01s`, two `down TimeoutError in 2.00s`, `unpaused`, `after unpause call 1: up in 0.00s`, `up at call 1`. **Probe calls until `up`: 1** (not more than two), so the design's "client per call" fallback is NOT taken; the probe keeps one dedicated, long-lived client built on its first call. (Observation only: why the cancelled call leaves the client usable was not investigated.) The two-successive-loops case: the probe is built in `start_runtime` (the lifespan's loop) and starts its client on the first call; Orders', Notifications' and the Projector's existing `test_or8_the_runtime_starts_and_stops_in_two_successive_event_loops` now call `readiness()` in each loop, so a client shared across loops fails them (arm `3_probe_client_shared_across_loops`: `AssertionError: assert (['factStream'], ...) == ([], ...)`).

## Unpause guarantee ("unpaused in a `finally`")

How it is guaranteed: (1) the only `.pause()` call in the repository's tests is inside `health_support.paused`, a context manager whose body is `try: yield / finally: reload; if paused: unpause`; `tests/architecture/test_pause_confinement.py` counts the calls over every test tree and the root `conftest.py` by AST (expected set a literal, the rest by subtraction; sentinels: a comment / a string is not a call, a call in another test file is found, a call under `if TYPE_CHECKING:` is counted; blind spot recorded: `getattr(c, "pause")()` and an aliased bound method are not calls of `.pause`) and checks the `unpause` is in the `finalbody` of the `try`; (2) `tests/observability/test_health_support.py` runs it against a REAL private NATS: a body that raises, a body that times out (`asyncio.timeout` inside the pause) and a body that completes each leave the container `running`; (3) every readiness test runs on a private container (module-scoped fixture, `with` block stops it), never the session's. **Armed**: arm `6_unpause_not_in_finally` (the `try/finally` replaced by sequential statements) failed `test_s6_a_body_that_raises_inside_paused_leaves_the_container_running` with `AssertionError: S6: a body that raised left the container paused: the unpause is not in a finally`; after the arms `docker ps -a --filter status=paused` was empty. The pacing of `poll` was armed by a change of kind (a read that fails in microseconds, budget 0.6 s, interval 0.25 s: `sleep(0)` in place of the pacing sleep made 302 574 reads in 0.600 s; the test asserts at most 4 reads and gaps of at least 0.24 s).

The `2_confine_*` arms ran `lint-imports` (`Contracts: 12 kept, 1 broken.`, the `fact-producer-confinement` contract BROKEN) and the confinement test (fails) with a producer import under `otc_orders/application/ports/clock.py` and an aliased `AIOKafkaAdminClient` import in `otc_orders/presentation/app.py`; the unlisted-probe arm added an untracked `services/gateway/src/otc_gateway/infrastructure/health/redis_probe.py` (an unbounded `RedisProbe`), failed `test_or6_every_probe_runs_under_a_bound` with `gateway/redis_probe.py/RedisProbe: a probe class that is not in EXPECTED_PROBES`, and was removed (an untracked file I created). The NATS probe changed after the first arms (`is_connected` replaced closed/reconnecting, see Deviation 3), so the NATS-touching arms were re-run: `e_*` and `f_*` rows.

- `tasks.md` S6.1 - S6.8 ticked; `R60` written DONE in `specs/shared/test-matrix.md` (every test name in the cell was checked to exist); `design.md` 11 rows L31 and L32 updated with the delivered guards.

## Deviations

1. **Each probe is bounded twice** (design 7.2: "each under `asyncio.timeout`"): inside the probe (`asyncio.timeout(self._bound)`; PostgreSQL also `connect_args={"timeout": ...}`, NATS also `flush(timeout=)`) and by `HealthCheck.run`. A single bound around the probe in `HealthCheck.run` would make "remove the bound in each kind" unarmable (the check's bound would still hold), and the unit cases call the probe directly.
2. **Fulfillment's and Billing's relay task is attributed to `writeModel`** (design 7.2 attributes "consumer, relay and `DlqDepthTask`" to `factStream`, but their table has no fact-stream check by design): the outbox is a table of the write model; the check keeps its name and the detail names the task (`transport task outbox-relay is not running`).
3. **NATS is `is_connected`**, not `is_closed` / `is_reconnecting`: the client's own truth, what the Gateway's `ping_nats` checked (`test_a_nats_connection_that_is_not_connected_is_down_even_if_flush_would_buffer`), and it is also false while reconnecting or connecting. The detail is `NatsNotConnectedError`; the old Projector reason strings "the NATS connection is closed" / "is reconnecting" are no longer separate. (My first version had two subclasses and was replaced when the Gateway's fakes failed; the arms touching NATS were re-run, rows `e_*`, `f_*`.)
4. **`/health/ready` before the lifespan has started (and after it has ended) is `{"status":"down","checks":{}}`**: no check exists to name, and `HealthResponse` has no top-level detail. The old body said `"the service has not started"`.
5. **`nats_probe.flush(timeout=math.ceil(bound))`**: the nats client takes an `int`; the authoritative bound is the outer `asyncio.timeout`.
6. **`OrdersRuntime.engine`** added (an attribute; Fulfillment, Billing and Notifications already exposed theirs): the write-after-unpause assertion must go through the service's own pool.
7. **`test_gateway_degraded.py`'s paused-NATS case** (feature 25) was moved onto `paused` so that the confinement census holds with ONE `.pause()` call; its assertions are unchanged.
8. **Projector `test_pr43_start_runtime_runs_its_steps_in_order`**: with the fake externals there is no broker to probe, so `unready_reasons() == []` became "no check reports a task reason" (`[list(check.task_reasons()) for check in runtime.checks] == [[], [], []]`).
9. **S6.4's Gateway leg** is not a new `test_gateway_readiness.py`: `test_gateway_health.py` and `test_gateway_composition.py` already drive each of its three checks down alone (cited in the task box).

## Findings and proposed dispositions

1. **My own slicing script duplicated a region of the Projector's `composition.py`** (it cut from the first `def unready_reasons` to the first `async def stop`, and a Protocol earlier in the file has an `async def stop`). `ruff check` named it (F811, redefinition of `Externals`); repaired before anything ran, verified one `class Externals` / `class ProjectorRuntime` / `def readiness` and a green mypy. The same edit on the other four compositions was checked the same way. Not a product defect; recorded as the reason a lint step runs after every scripted edit.
2. **A dead consumer's detail carries two reasons** (the task is done AND the consumer has left its group), joined by `; `. Accept: both are true, and the check name is the claim.
3. **Detail of a paused PostgreSQL / MongoDB / NATS is `no answer within 2.0 s` whichever bound fires first** (asyncpg's connect timeout, the probe's `asyncio.timeout`, the check's): the three are equal on purpose. Accept.
4. **`KafkaAdminProbe.aclose` waits at most 5 s** (`CLOSE_BOUND_SECONDS`) on a paused broker, which adds at most that to a shutdown during an outage. Accept.
5. **The Gateway's `streamSignals` check** is outside the four names `HealthResponse.checks` documents (`openapi.yaml` leaves the keys free). Existing (feature 25), kept. Accept.
6. **The pause-confinement census cannot see `getattr(container, "pause")()` or an aliased bound method** (it counts `Call(Attribute(attr in {pause, unpause}))`); recorded in the test's sentinel, not hidden. Accept; a second instrument would be a string search, which the defeat list says loses.
7. **`describe_cluster` answered in 0.00 s on the first call after the unpause** in the measurement; I did not look for why a cancelled request does not leave the client in a bad state. Re-open only if a later release changes the behaviour (the two-loops and paused-Kafka cases run on every full run).
8. **Runtime cost**: the six modules start 4 PostgreSQL, 5 NATS, 3 Kafka and 2 MongoDB containers, once per module (not per case); the first five modules (12 cases) ran in 62 s together, the Fulfillment module (2 cases) in 9.9 s.

## Ledger

- L31 (`Readiness probe cannot hang`): guards `test_health_probe_bounds.py` (AST, armed) + the per-kind units + the paused integration cases; row updated in `design.md` 11.
- L32 (`A cancelled probe does not poison a pool`): `NullPool` probe engine; the integration case writes through the service's pool before the pause and after the unpause; row updated. Armed? The poisoned-pool failure needs a pooled probe, which this design does not have; the claim is the absence of the pooled probe, and the write-after-unpause assertion is a regression guard, not an armed one. Stated here rather than ticked as armed.
- L7/L12/L13 unaffected. No other row changed.
