### S7.7 -- the L32 arming attempt (leader, S6 checkpoint)

Probe: the mutation target is the claim "the readiness probe engine uses `NullPool`". In Fulfillment's `postgres_probe.py` (4 services carry the copy; one is enough) `poolclass=NullPool` was removed (`create_async_engine(url, connect_args={"timeout": bound_seconds})`, SQLAlchemy's default `AsyncAdaptedQueuePool`), then `services/fulfillment/tests/integration/test_fulfillment_health_probes.py` and `services/fulfillment/tests/unit` were run: `1 failed, 331 passed`. The failing case (re-run alone):

```
FAILED ...test_fulfillment_health_probes.py::test_r60_readiness_reports_the_paused_dependency_down_within_its_bound_while_liveness_answers_throughout_and_recovers[writeModel]
E               asyncio.exceptions.CancelledError
E                   TimeoutError
E           AssertionError: not within 7.5 s: readiness never reported writeModel down while its container was paused (after 0 reads, last [])
```

Reading: with a pooled probe, the bound's cancellation of the probe does not return; it blocks on the paused connection (the pool's connection is cancelled mid-query and cleaned up through a protocol that waits for the paused server), so the readiness request never answers `down`. The S6 record said L32 could not be armed because the design has no pooled probe; that was a claim about the absence of a mutation target, and the absence IS the mutation target. Restored by `cp` (cmp ok), caches cleared, `2 passed` (9.87 s); no container left paused (`docker ps -a --filter status=paused`: empty). **L32 is armed**; the existing case is the guard (no direct "next probe answers up after a cancelled probe" test is owed: the same test's recovery leg runs after the unpause). `design.md` section 11 row L32 updated.

### Stage gate (developer stack down, only `otcpy-n8n` running)

- `ruff format --check`: `1184 files already formatted`; `ruff check`: `All checks passed!`; `mypy`: `Success: no issues found in 968 source files` (no new override); `lint-imports`: `Contracts: 13 kept, 0 broken.`; `./init.sh`: exit 0.
- Whole workspace (`.arm/r27/s7/gate.xml`): `tests="5207" failures="2"`, 828.4 s. The two failures were consequences of this stage, both fixed in the stage: `test_no_reload_servers.py::test_item_7_the_population_...` (the new `test_gateway_shutdown_with_open_streams.py` is a 13th server site: the guard did its job; the literal gained it) and `test_gateway_listens_on_gateway_port.py::test_without_gateway_port_...` (its AST test looked for the first `.run` call and so for `uvicorn.run`'s kwargs; it now reads the `uvicorn.Config` call).
- After the fixes: `tests/architecture` + `services/gateway` (`.arm/r27/s7/rerun.xml`): `tests="1129" failures="0"`, 180.5 s; the edited AST test re-run alone: 1 passed. Net against S6 (5 188): +19 tests (16 guard + 1 OR11 + 1 OR13 + 1 hub), 5 207 in the whole run.

### Deviations

1. **S7.3 arm 1 as worded survives** (above); armed as the combination. Reported, not ticked as the plain form.
2. **`log_config=None`** on the Gateway's `uvicorn.Config` (not in the design): required, because `Config.__init__` configures logging (verified by reading the uvicorn source: `Config.__init__` -> `configure_logging`) and would replace the R58 JSON handler. The Gateway log-contract test (`Started server process` is JSON) is the regression guard.
3. **The Gateway's app object, not an import string**, goes to `uvicorn.Config` (as the design says), so `uvicorn --reload`/`--workers` semantics (which need an import string) are unavailable through `python -m otc_gateway`: unchanged intent (one worker, per-process limit state).
4. The OR13 test asserts all claims together in one failure message (so the arm shows the stream, exit-time and ERROR-line claims at once) rather than failing at the first.

### Findings and proposed dispositions

1. **S6's "L32 cannot be armed" was wrong** (leader's catch); now armed. Accept as fixed.
2. **The OR13 test measures only a clean path**: two idle streams with no queued frames. A stream mid-write at the signal is the same code path (`Subscription.get` returns `None` after draining), proven at unit level by `test_or13_end_all_...` (queued frame still delivered). Accept.
3. **`python -m otc_gateway` exits with `-15` or `0`** depending on how uvicorn re-raises the captured signal; the test accepts both, as the existing port test does. Accept.
4. **`StreamHub.end_all` is permanent** for that hub (`_ended`): a hub is never reused after the exit signal. Accept.

### Findings for the leader (not fixed here: not defects of the feature's files, or a decision)

- **F1 (L2, Gateway).** Since S7 the Gateway's `python -m otc_gateway` passes `log_config=None`; removing the uvicorn re-propagation loop from the Gateway's `logging.py` copy no longer fails `test_gateway_log_contract.py` (one-off run, restored by `cp`/`cmp`, backup `.arm/r27/s8a/gateway_logging.bak`, sha256 `10a4e353a873...`). The step is still needed, and guarded, for the five services started with `python -m uvicorn` (S1.6's Fulfillment arm re-run is not needed: file unchanged). The Gateway's by-hand `uvicorn otc_gateway.main:app` path (documented in `__main__.py`) is unguarded. Proposed: accept; ledger L2 says so.
- **F2 (L4).** The Orders relay (and the Fulfillment and Billing copies), the command dispatcher and (through the dispatcher) the fast path bind `correlationId` AND pass `extra={"correlationId": ...}` on every line they write: the binding is redundant for those lines and a deleted binding changes no rendered line. The census is the only guard (S1 finding 1 stands for these sites). Proposed: accept with this sentence, or drop the `extra` argument at those sites so the binding is what is tested (a source change; the S3.5 deviation 1 already moved the relay's site once).
- **F3 (L6).** After S4.21 the S3.10 test no longer fails when the fast-path task is created with an empty context (the dispatch span restores `saga_commands.trace_parent`). It guards the observable property; `test_so14_...` (feature 16) guards the mechanism and was armed with the same mutation. Proposed: accept; ledger row reworded.
- **F4 (S8.2 case 201, `KafkaGroupHostWrappingTests`).** #8 asserts structurally that every test host that joins a consumer group is wrapped for clearance. #9's harnesses delete the group per test (`saga/conftest.py`) and S4.1 enumerated every host-starting teardown (49 modules, record S4a), but no executable guard asserts that a consumer-starting test goes through a clearing harness. Proposed: accept (the private-topic seam makes a leaked group harmless for correctness) or add an AST guard over the enumeration; a decision, not done here.
- **F5 (S8.2 cases 145, 154).** No unit test asserts that `SagaFact.raw` / the topic reach `OwedCommand`; the integration case compares the stored bytes with the bytes Kafka delivered, so the chain is guarded end to end (armed 4.11a, 4.11c, re-run). Proposed: accept.
- **F6 (counts).** `tasks.md` S8.1 says 40 rows; `design.md` 11 holds 41 (`sed -n '429,480p' specs/observability_reliability/design.md | grep -c '^| L[0-9]'` -> 41; L41 was added in S4b). S8.2's 223 reconciles as 219 added + 4 modified (below).
- **F7 (outside the feature).** `services/billing/src/otc_billing/infrastructure/outbox/payloads.py:10` says `payment.received.v1` "has no caller until feature 22"; not this feature's wording (the S8.5 pattern is `feature 27`), likely stale. Not touched.
- **F8 (survivor, known).** `4.3d2` (`except BaseException` against the cancelled-back-off case) still survives, by construction, as ledger L10 states; the back-off half is armed by 4.3d3.

### S8.5 — retired wording

Command (the brief's, from the repository root) and its output after the rewrite:

```
$ grep -rn 'feature 27\|until feature 27\|dead-lettered by feature 27\|Feature 27 wraps' services/*/src packages/*/src
$ echo $?
1
```

(`ugrep`; exit 1 = no match.) Before: 31 lines in 31 files, all `feature 27`; a wider case-insensitive search (`grep -rniE 'feature[ -]?27'`) found one more, `Feature 27 (migration 0003)` in `orders/.../persistence/models.py:210`. The sweep sentinel: a temporary tree with `# kept for feature 27` in `services/x/src/a.py` -> `services/x/src/a.py:1:# kept for feature 27` (the pattern detects). Classification of the 32 hits and the rewrite of each:

| Hits | Wording | Rewritten to |
|---|---|---|
| 19 (`metrics.py`, `telemetry.py`, `trace_context.py` in the six services, and Orders' `logging.py`) | `... feature 27 `design.md` N` (a citation of where the design lives, not a promise) | `the spec's `design.md` N` (the first line had to stay under 100 columns; the copy-parity instrument compares after the docstring, so the six copies stay equal) |
| 3 x `application/ports/fact_stream.py` (Orders, Projector, Notifications) | `# kept for feature 27's traceparent` (a promise: now stale, the consume span reads it) | `# the consume span's parent is read from `traceparent`` |
| 3 x `infrastructure/messaging/kafka_fact_subscriber.py` | `Since feature 27 the handler ... retries a processing failure itself and dead-letters it` (true, but a feature number) | `Since `R16` the handler ...` |
| `notification/degrading_sender.py:9` | `feature 27's retry and dead-letter` | `the fact retry and dead letter of `R16`` |
| `health/kafka_probe.py:11` (Orders) | `measured in feature 27's S6.3` | `measured in `observability_reliability` S6.3` |
| `presentation/saga_facts_consumer.py:24` | `(feature 27, `R16`)` | `(`R16`)` |
| `messaging/nats_saga_commands.py:22` | `` `traceparent` is feature 27's.`` (a promise: now stale) | `` `traceparent` is injected per call (`OR4`).`` |
| `persistence/mongo_read_model_writer.py:15` (Projector) | `a poison record that is feature 27's to dead-letter` (a promise: now stale) | `a poison record that the fact retry dead-letters (`R16`)` |
| `saga/fast_path.py:13` | `so feature 27's span context and `correlationId` log binding reach the dispatch` | `so the trace context and the `correlationId` log binding reach the dispatch` |
| `persistence/models.py:210` | `# Feature 27 (migration 0003): ...` | `# `observability_reliability` (migration 0003): ...` |

After the rewrite: `uv run ruff format` / `ruff check` clean (three lines re-wrapped), `mypy` green, `tests/architecture/test_observability_copy_parity.py` + `test_outbox_copy_parity.py` + `test_correlation_binding_sites.py`: 140 passed, then the whole `tests/architecture` directory: 696 passed (before the tests added below).

Re-run decisions were made by comparing the `sha256 before` of every arm log under `.arm/logs/` with the file now (a docstring-only difference was excluded by reversing my own S8.5 edit and comparing again: the nine subscriber arms are unchanged), and by file mtimes against the arm times of the S2/S3 stages (their arms predate the logs directory). Files that changed after their arm: `fact_retry.py` (S4a arms, F1 changed it), the three composition roots (S4b arms, then S5/S6), `fact_handler.py` / `fact_command_handlers.py` (S5.5), `unit_of_work.py` (S3.8, after S2's arms), `relay.py` and Gateway `asgi.py` (S5), the six `presentation/app.py` (S6), the Gateway entry point (S7), the dispatch span in `command_dispatcher.py` (S4.21, which changed what the fast path's trace depends on).


**Verdicts.** Of the 41 rows: **34 ticked** (the table says which of them re-ran an arm), **4 ticked after a fix or a first arm made in this pass** (L9, L13, L19, L40), and **3 reported, not ticked** (L2 for the Gateway only, L4 for the relay x3 and dispatcher sites only, L6). Every other row's guard executes its subject and has an arm in an earlier section of this record, cited by box.


Legend. **Armed** = the record entry whose mutation was seen to fail; **re-ran** = armed again in this pass because a later stage changed a file on the arm's path (decided by comparing each arm log's `sha256 before` with the file now, and by file mtimes against the stage's arm times; a docstring-only difference does not count). **Executes?** is the answer to "does this test execute the code the row is about?". Verdict: **ticked**, **ticked after fix** (a defect found in this walk, fixed and armed in S8a), or **REPORTED** (not ticked).

### S8a arms (the table behind the verdicts)

Tool `.arm/arm.py` for every row; backups in `.arm/bak/<arm id>/`; the full run of each in `.arm/logs/<arm id>.log`; the logs the re-runs overwrote are kept in `.arm/r27/s8a/old_logs/` (19 files). "First failing line" is the first `E` line of the mutated run, verbatim; `s8a-2.5` ends with `AssertionError: an order_reference collision was translated as a request-id collision` (the first lines are the chained exceptions). One-off arms run by hand: the Gateway propagation loop (`gateway_logging.bak`, sha256 `10a4e353a873...`, run `-k every_line`: `1 passed`, restored, `cmp` identical) and the three `uv.lock` arms (backup `uv.lock.bak`, sha256 `4f1c3a6e...`; restored, `cmp` identical).

### S8.2 — one line per #8 case

**Population (by command).** The extractor `.arm/r27/s8a/extract_8.py` (kept) lists every `[Fact]` / `[Theory]` the five commits ADDED to `tests/*.cs` and the public method that follows:

```
$ cd ../order-to-cash-dotnet && python3 .arm/r27/s8a/extract_8.py . d8d71c7 e58ea63 6c35084 55b21f7 ad00f79
219 Counter({'d8d71c7': 194, 'ad00f79': 19, 'e58ea63': 3, '6c35084': 2, '55b21f7': 1}) 80
```

219 is not the 223 `design.md` 12 and the task cite. The difference is exactly four EXISTING cases that `d8d71c7` modified without adding their attribute line (the design's extractor listed the methods inside a hunk): `ProjectorConsumesOnlyTests.PR21_...` (renamed), `DocsAndAnonymousRouteHttpTests.EveryRegisteredRouteExceptTheFourDocumentedPublicOnes_...`, `OrdersCreateAcceptanceTests.OrdersCreate_AFirstTimeRequestIdOnTheWirePlacesAnOrderNormally`, `SeedIntegrationTests.TheRelayFindsNoUnpublishedRecordInAnySeededWriteModel` (cases 220 – 223 below; their names read from the diff hunks). `d8d71c7` therefore adds 194 + modifies 4 = 198, as the design says. **219 + 4 = 223.**

**Existence check, by script** (`.arm/r27/s8a/check_s8_2.py`, run against `uv run pytest --collect-only -q`, output kept in `.arm/r27/s8a/collect.txt`):

```
5216 tests collected in 2.66s
cases extracted: 223 | dispositions: 223 | by kind: {'P': 147, 'NA': 58, 'S8a': 4, 'PI': 4, 'D': 4, 'PN': 1, 'E': 5}
delivered-name references: 183 | resolved: 183 | distinct #9 tests: 143
collected test functions (distinct, parameters stripped): 2713
missing: []
ambiguous: []
extra dispositions: []
checker-exit=0
```

Sentinel (a sweep needs a case that must fail to detect): with one delivered name misspelled (`..._the_final_failureX`, in `.arm/r27/s8a/sentinel/map8.py`) the checker prints `missing: ['case 112: orders:test_fact_retry.py::test_or1_the_header_instants_are_the_first_and_the_final_failureX']` and exits 1. A name counts as existing only if exactly one collected test function (parameters stripped) matches the file and the function under the stated service (or `tests/architecture`).

Kinds: **P** ported (a #9 test proves the same claim), **PI** ported inverted per gate G1 (no synthetic cancel envelope, no dead letter), **PN** ported in kind (the mechanism differs), **E** carried by an earlier feature's existing test, **S8a** a gap this walk found, closed here, **NA** not applicable (reason given), **D** deliberately not ported (reason given). A #8 case whose claim a #9 test proves only as one of several claims of that test says so in the last column; where two #9 tests prove two halves of one #8 case both are named (cases 11, 23, 110). Cases 62 – 64 and 167 – 169 share one #9 test each (one broker state / one parametrised test); cases 130 and 131 share one (the fakes raise if called).

## S8b — F7, the live walkthrough (S8.3), `quality.sh` (S8.6)

Implementer pass, 2026-10-13. Verbatim command log of the walkthrough: `.arm/r27/s8b/walk.log` (345 lines; host logs, trace JSON, the dead-letter reader and the poison producer beside it in `.arm/r27/s8b/`). `quality.sh` log: `.arm/r27/s8b/quality.log`.

### F7 — the stale "no caller until feature 22"

`services/billing/src/otc_billing/infrastructure/outbox/payloads.py` (module docstring only). The caller, found with `grep -rn "payment_received\|PaymentReceived\|payment.received" services/billing/src`: `PaymentReceived` is raised and returned by `Invoice.mark_paid` (`domain/invoice.py:353-372`), which the payment-register handler calls (`application/payment_register.py:147`). The sentence now says so (wrapped at 100 columns; `ruff format --check` and `ruff check` clean on the file). No code changed.

### S8.3 — the live walkthrough (design §16)

**Preconditions, with commands.** `docker ps` before: `otcpy-n8n` only. `docker compose -p otcpy -f docker-compose.infra.yml start postgres mongodb kafka nats otel-collector jaeger prometheus mailpit` (all started, mailpit included per the leader's correction). Kafka: a bounded paced loop on `docker inspect -f '{{.State.Health.Status}}' otcpy-kafka` reported `healthy` on try 1; postgres, mongodb, nats `healthy`. The developer `.env` carried the three new keys (`FACT_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3`, `FACT_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS=500`, `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=http://localhost:4318`, read with `grep`).

**Parked rows counted before starting Orders** (`select status, count(*) from saga_commands group by 1`): `rejected 1` (ORD-000020 `stock.release`, `PRECONDITION_FAILED`, the operator-cancel overtaken by the despatch), `sent 53`; `pending`/`parked`: **0 rows**. **Expected `order.saga_failed.v1` appended on start: 0.** Observed after the walkthrough: `select count(*) from outbox where event_type='order.saga_failed.v1'` = **0** (the `rejected` row is never retried or parked, so §5.6's hook does not fire).

**Migration.** `uv run alembic -c services/orders/alembic.ini current` = `0002`; `... upgrade head`; `current` = `0003 (head)`; `alembic_version` = `0003`; the four columns exist with the types the design names (`triggering_event_envelope bytea`, `triggering_event_topic varchar`, `dead_lettered_at timestamptz`, `trace_parent varchar`). Fulfillment, Billing, Notifications: `0001 (head)`, untouched.

**Hosts.** Orders 8101, Fulfillment 8102, Billing 8103, Projector 8105 (`uv run uvicorn otc_<svc>.main:app --port N`), Notifications 8104 with `NOTIFICATIONS_SENDER=smtp`, the Gateway `uv run python -m otc_gateway` (3001). `setsid` without `--wait` forks, so `$!` is `setsid`'s PID and `uv`'s; the server PIDs were taken from `ss -ltnp "sport = :<port>"` and recorded in `.arm/r27/s8b/pids_real` (orders 3473364, fulfillment 3473362, billing 3473363, notifications 3473367, projector 3473365, gateway 3473366). Every `/health/ready` answered 200 within 1-2 tries (bounded loop).

**Step 1 — run.** Login (`POST /auth/login`, token 208 chars). A first attempt with a non-UUID `Idempotency-Key` returned `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` ("Input should be a valid UUID"): the header is a UUID; my typo, not a defect. `POST /orders` twice with `Idempotency-Key: ec28809b-3af9-4e56-a50d-c5abee009d6d` and the same body: both `201`, **both `orderId` `a4ea79cb-401a-4099-affc-2b466baa77ab`, `ORD-000022`**, byte-identical bodies, `x-correlation-id` equal to the order id. (One call of mine duplicated the `Authorization` header and drew a 401 from both; recorded in the log, no state changed.)

**Step 2 — run.** Jaeger `GET /api/traces?service=gateway`: trace `51c646a4e96777fe960604029b942fdf`, **46 spans, five services** (gateway, orders, fulfillment, billing, projector). Counts by service / operation / kind: gateway `POST /orders` server 1, `orders.create request` client 1; orders `orders.create serve` server 1, `writemodel.transaction` 7, `fulfillment.stock.check request` 1, `outbox.publish` producer 2, `saga.command.dispatch` 4, `fulfillment.stock.reserve request`, `billing.credit.hold request`, `fulfillment.despatch.create request`, `billing.invoice.issue request` 1 each, consumer spans `otc.{orders,fulfillment,billing}.facts.v1 process` 2 each; fulfillment `stock.check`/`stock.reserve`/`despatch.create serve` 1 each, `writemodel.transaction` 2, `outbox.publish` 2, ...; billing `credit.hold`/`invoice.issue serve`, `writemodel.transaction` 2, `outbox.publish` 2; projector consumer spans 2 per topic. The replay (second POST) is its own 4-span trace (`6d629d6f...`, gateway + orders). Required set present: gateway server span, `orders.create` request and serve, `writemodel.transaction`, `outbox.publish`, consumers in other services.

**Step 3 — run.** To make the saga-completion series non-empty the order was carried to `completed`: `POST /invoices/41667596-.../payments` with `paymentReference` `LIVE27-S8B-1`, `source` `operator` (a first attempt with `source: manual` drew a `400`, enum is `operator|robot|test`): `201 accepted`, order `completed`. Prometheus (after 45 s): `otc_request_latency_ms_count` by `exported_job` {gateway 6}; `otc_fact_processing_latency_ms_count` by `exported_job,consumer` {notifications 17, orders.saga 7, projector 9}; `otc_saga_completion_ms_count` {orders, outcome completed, 1}; `otc_outbox_lag_ms` {billing 0, fulfillment 0, orders 0}; `otc_dlq_depth` {orders: `otc.billing.facts.v1.dlq` 0, `otc.fulfillment.facts.v1.dlq` 3, `otc.orders.facts.v1.dlq` 14}. All five non-empty, each with `exported_job`. (The DLQ depths are the pollution of F8 below, which the gauge reported correctly.)

**Step 4 — run.** A throwaway producer (`.arm/r27/s8b/poison.py`, not product code) published to `otc.orders.facts.v1` a valid envelope (`eventId 660b02f3-d0bd-4313-b72b-5bc3f2f10415`, key `c344c4de-...`, partition 2 offset 23) with `payload {"orderReference":"ORD-POISON","bogus":true}`. Both consumers of that topic dead-lettered it to `otc.orders.facts.v1.dlq` (partition 2, offsets 9 and 10), key equal to the original, value byte-identical, headers (read with aiokafka, `.arm/r27/s8b/readdlq.py`; the console consumer splits the multi-line `x-error`): `x-failed-consumer` (`projector` / `orders.saga`), `x-attempts=3`, `x-error` (`ValidationError: 11 validation errors for OrderPlacedEvent ...`), `x-original-topic=otc.orders.facts.v1`, `x-first-failed-at=2026-10-13T00:50:50.472Z`, `x-failed-at=2026-10-13T00:50:51.975Z`, `x-event-type`, `content-type`, `traceparent` (a different trace id per consumer). `kafka-console-consumer` is available in the broker image as `/opt/kafka/bin/kafka-console-consumer.sh`. Orders and Projector logged one `error` line each (the dead letter) and three `warning` lines (the attempts), no other error. The 24 redelivered copies of 8 events did not reach any `.dlq` from the Projector or the saga (their logs hold no error before the poison; the 17 existing dead letters all carry `x-failed-consumer: notifications`).

**Step 5 — run.** Billing `/health/ready` `200` (`writeModel up`, `rpcTransport up`), `/health/live` `200`. `docker pause otcpy-postgres`: `/health/ready` **`503`** `{"status":"down","checks":{"writeModel":{"status":"down","detail":"no answer within 2.0 s"},"rpcTransport":{"status":"up"}}}` on try 1, `/health/live` still `200 {"status":"up"}`. `docker unpause`: `/health/ready` `200` on the next try. Not run: a second paused dependency (NATS, Kafka).

**Not run:** the `unpause` was the only recovery step for step 5 beyond the above; no browser. Everything else in §16 was run.

**F8 (finding, not fixed; for the leader).** Notifications started with `NOTIFICATIONS_SENDER=smtp` against Mailpit and the developer `.env`'s `SMTP_USER=` / `SMTP_PASSWORD=` (blank, as `.env.example` ships them) failed every send with `SMTPException: The SMTP AUTH extension is not supported by this server`; after three attempts each lagged fact was **dead-lettered: 17 records** (14 on `otc.orders.facts.v1.dlq`, 3 on `otc.fulfillment.facts.v1.dlq`, all `x-failed-consumer: notifications`) within 4 s. I stopped Notifications by PID 29 s after its start (the leader's mailpit correction did not prevent this: the sink was up, the credentials were the problem). Reading, not proved: the blank dotenv values arrive as `""`, not `None`, and `SmtpSender.send` passes `username=settings.user` through (`smtp_sender.py:36`), so aiosmtplib tries AUTH. Proposed disposition: fix in feature 23's settings (map a blank `SMTP_USER`/`SMTP_PASSWORD` to `None`, with a test that arms it) or document the keys as "unset, not blank"; the 17 dead letters stay on the dev topics (register below). It also means Notifications' consumer/DLQ path was exercised live by accident: the dead-letter records carry the same header set as step 4.

## S8c — fix pass for F8 and F9

### F8 (feature-23 defect, fixed in feature 27): blank SMTP credentials attempted AUTH

`SmtpSettings` read `SMTP_USER=` / `SMTP_PASSWORD=` (as shipped in `.env.example:177-178`) as `''`; the pair rule treated `''` as unset but `smtp_sender.py` passed `''` to aiosmtplib, which attempted AUTH; Mailpit answered "SMTP AUTH extension not supported". Fix: `SmtpSettings._a_blank_credential_is_unset`, a `field_validator("user", "password", mode="before")` mapping a blank (whitespace-only) string to `None` in `services/notifications/src/otc_notifications/infrastructure/settings.py`.

Ported-idiom row: #7 relied on `isConfigured` trimming and treating blank / `replace_me` as unset (`../order-to-cash-nestjs/apps/notifications/src/infrastructure/notification/smtp.config.ts:42-48`, used at 54-55; `replace_me` is deliberately not ported: not a #9 value); #8 supplied it by a transport that never authenticates (`../order-to-cash-dotnet/src/Notifications/Infrastructure/Notification/MailKitSmtpTransport.cs:9-12`, the class header); in #9 it is supplied by normalising a blank credential to `None` at the settings boundary, so the pair rule and the sender agree.

Tests: `services/notifications/tests/unit/test_notifications_settings_env.py::test_a_blank_or_whitespace_smtp_credential_is_none_and_a_real_pair_is_kept`; `services/notifications/tests/integration/test_blank_smtp_credentials_real_smtp.py::test_blank_smtp_credentials_deliver_one_message_to_a_real_mailpit` (real Mailpit, `SmtpSettings()` from the environment, real `aiosmtplib`). Arm: validator retargeted to a nonexistent field (backup in `.arm/`, restored by `cp`, `cmp` clean): the integration test failed with `aiosmtplib.errors.SMTPException: The SMTP AUTH extension is not supported by this server.`; the unit test failed with `AssertionError: ''`. Restored, green.

### B1 — the first-park dead letter's `x-first-failed-at` (SA-3)

**Built.** Migration `0003` (uncommitted, edited in place) gained a fifth nullable column `saga_commands.first_failed_at timestamptz(3)`; `models.py` mirrors it. The two ledger writes that record a failed attempt, `SqlAlchemySagaCommandLedger.park` and `.reject`, take `first_failed_at` and write `COALESCE(first_failed_at, :given)`. Sites by search (`grep -n "update(SagaCommand)"` over `command_ledger.py`, `command_queue.py`): `try_claim`, `claim_due`, `mark_sent` record no attempt; `park` and `reject` do; `claim_first_park` (queue) only sets `dead_lettered_at`. The dispatcher reads `self._clock.now()` inside the first `except SagaCommandError` of its in-line loop (kept in `first_failed_at`) and hands it to `park`, or to `reject` (the first failure of the dispatch, or the rejection's own instant when the rejection is the first failure). `claim_first_park` returns it (`FirstParkClaim.first_failed_at`); `first_park.py` sends it as `x-first-failed-at` and the park instant as `x-failed-at`.

**A row with an envelope and no `first_failed_at`: cannot arise (verified by reading).** The envelope is written only at enqueue by a build that has `0003`; the claim requires `status = 'parked'`, and the only statement that makes a row `parked` is `park`, which writes `first_failed_at` in the same statement. A dispatch interrupted before its park persists no attempt (`attempts`/`last_error` are written only by `park`/`reject`), so no earlier failure is lost. A row parked before `0003` has no envelope, so no dead letter is owed for it (OR3 (b)); its next `park` stores `first_failed_at`. The code falls back to the park instant for a NULL (`claim.first_failed_at or now`): unreachable by construction, untested, and recorded as such in design §5.6.

**Files.** `services/orders/alembic/versions/0003_saga_commands_dead_letter.py`; `src/otc_orders/infrastructure/persistence/models.py`; `.../saga/command_ledger.py`, `command_queue.py`, `command_dispatcher.py`, `first_park.py`; `.../application/ports/saga_command_store.py`. Tests: `tests/unit/saga/test_command_dispatcher.py` (fake ledger kwargs; `TickingClock`; `test_r29_sa3_the_park_carries_the_instant_of_the_first_failed_attempt_not_the_last`, `test_r29_sa3_a_terminal_rejection_after_a_transient_failure_carries_the_first_failure`); `tests/integration/saga/test_saga_command_ledger.py` (`test_sa3_the_first_failed_attempts_instant_is_recorded_once_and_a_later_failure_never_overwrites_it`, distinct fixture instants; every `park`/`reject` call passes `FIRST_FAILED`), `test_saga_command_rejected_ledger.py` (reject stores it); `test_saga_first_park_dead_letter.py` (below); `tests/integration/test_orders_schema_types.py` and `test_orders_migration_lifecycle.py` (the column joins both census literals); `tests/integration/saga/conftest.py` (`command_rows` selects it). Spec: design §5.6 (table, item 2, delivery note 5, the no-NULL paragraph), §11 L17 and L36 notes, §13 id 75 ("RECURRED on the first-park path, found in review round 1"), §14 D6.

**The `:211` assertion replaced.** `test_r29_the_first_park_…` wraps the stand-in's `silent` behaviour to note the wall-clock instant each request ARRIVED (`arrivals`), then asserts `arrivals[0] (ms-truncated) <= x-first-failed-at < arrivals[1]` (attempt 1 fails after its 400 ms timeout and attempt 2 arrives after the back-off), `x-first-failed-at < x-failed-at`, and `x-first-failed-at ==` the stored `first_failed_at`. The old `x-first-failed-at == x-failed-at` is gone.

**Arms (all restored; `sha256sum -c .arm/r27-round2/shas1.txt` OK).**

### Surprises

- The first-park integration test uses the real `SystemClock`, so "distinct fixture instants" are the test's own observed request arrivals rather than injected ones; the bracket is `arrival_1 <= first <= arrival_2`, tight enough that the old both-instants-equal shape fails it.
- `x-first-failed-at` is also stored for a rejected row (cheap, symmetric with `park`); it is never sent for one (a rejection is not a first park).
